## SR13 · S4 — Every refusal reached through the `${ref:…}` spelling carries no location (kwargs on a plain value, self-reference, unresolvable miss, embedded, attribute), while the `!ref:` twin of each names its line

**Where:** `confluid/resolver.py:837` — `return Reference(path)` in `_marker_resolver` (called from `hoist_marker_placeholders`, `:431`) builds the marker with `_yaml_loc = None`; every later raise renders `_at_yaml_loc(ref)` as `""`: `:510-514` (fold), `confluid/engine.py:586-588` (self-referential), `:602-605` (miss), `confluid/resolver.py:374-379` (attribute), `:777-780` (embedded).

**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import load, configurable
@configurable
class S:
    def __init__(self, v=1): self.v = v
for label, doc in [
    ("${ref} kwargs plain  ", "proto: 5\nuse: ${ref:proto}\nuse.k: 1\n"),
    ("!ref kwargs plain    ", "proto: 5\nuse: !ref:proto\n  k: 1\n"),
    ("${ref} self          ", "x: ${ref:x}\n"),
    ("!ref self            ", "x: !ref:x\n"),
    ("${ref} miss          ", "use: ${ref:nope}\n"),
    ("!ref miss            ", "use: !ref:nope\n"),
    ("${ref} attr          ", "m: !class:S\nuse: ${ref:m.hidden}\n"),
    ("!ref attr            ", "m: !class:S\nuse: !ref:m.hidden\n"),
    ("${ref} embedded      ", "proto: 5\nuse: pre-${ref:proto}\n"),
]:
    try: load(doc, until="settled")
    except Exception as e:
        msg = str(e).replace("\n", " ")
        print(label, "->", type(e).__name__, "| located:", "<unicode string>" in msg, "|", msg[:95])
```
**Actual output:**
```
${ref} kwargs plain   -> ConfigurationError | located: False | !ref:proto carries kwargs ['k'], but `proto` is a plain value, not a marker — a reference's kwa
!ref kwargs plain     -> ConfigurationError | located: True | !ref:proto at <unicode string>:2:6 carries kwargs ['k'], but `proto` is a plain value, not a ma
${ref} self           -> ReferenceResolutionError | located: False | Self-referential !ref:x: the only 'x' in scope is this reference itself. Define a top-level 'x'
!ref self             -> ReferenceResolutionError | located: True | Self-referential !ref:x at <unicode string>:1:4: the only 'x' in scope is this reference itself
${ref} miss           -> ReferenceResolutionError | located: False | Cannot resolve !ref:nope: no key in scope, no structural path and no importable name matches it
!ref miss             -> ReferenceResolutionError | located: True | Cannot resolve !ref:nope at <unicode string>:1:6: no key in scope, no structural path and no im
${ref} attr           -> ConfigurationError | located: False | !ref:m.hidden reads the ATTRIBUTE `hidden` of the object built at `m` — attribute references we
!ref attr             -> ConfigurationError | located: True | !ref:m.hidden at <unicode string>:2:6 reads the ATTRIBUTE `hidden` of the object built at `m` —
${ref} embedded       -> ConfigurationError | located: False | ${ref:proto} cannot be embedded in a string — a reference must be the whole value (got 'pre-${r
```
**Expected:** a location on the `${ref:}` rows too — `docs/plain-format.md`: "`a: ${ref:proto}` … scalar shorthand … `b: {_ref_: proto}` … the mapping form, same thing"; `AGENTS.md`: "EVERY error raised while processing a document NAMES ITS `file:line:col`". The messages even spell the marker as `!ref:…`, which is not what the author wrote.
**Known?** overlaps the documented scalar-location limitation (`resolver.py:355-356`: "empty for the `${ref:...}` string spelling — a scalar carries no location"; `AGENTS.md` → "a STRING is never parsed as one"). Filed per the brief's rule 3; the FILE is knowable at the hoist site even where the line is not.

## SR14 · S3 — An import-path reference whose module fails to import raises the module's raw exception (`SyntaxError`, `RuntimeError`, …) — untyped and naming no config location

**Where:** `confluid/resolver.py:296-301` — `_import_base` catches ONLY `ImportError` (`try: return importlib.import_module(obj_path) except ImportError: … resolve_class(obj_path)`); anything else a module raises at import time escapes through `resolve_reference_path` (`:408`) and `engine._settle_reference` (`:599`) unwrapped.

**Repro:**
```python
from pathlib import Path
from confluid import load
Path("broken_mod.py").write_text("def f(:\n    pass\n")
Path("raising_mod.py").write_text('raise RuntimeError("import-time failure")\n')
for label, doc in [
    ("syntax error module ", "fn: !ref:broken_mod.f\n"),
    ("raising module      ", "fn: !ref:raising_mod.g\n"),
    ("missing module      ", "fn: !ref:no_such_mod.g\n"),
    ("missing attr        ", "fn: !ref:os.path.no_such_attr\n"),
    ("${ref} syntax error ", "fn: ${ref:broken_mod.f}\n"),
]:
    try: print(label, "->", load(doc, until="settled"))
    except Exception as e: print(label, "->", type(e).__name__, "| located:", "<unicode string>" in str(e), "|", str(e)[:110].replace("\n", " "))
```
**Actual output:**
```
syntax error module  -> SyntaxError | located: False | invalid syntax (broken_mod.py, line 1)
raising module       -> RuntimeError | located: False | import-time failure
missing module       -> ReferenceResolutionError | located: True | Cannot resolve !ref:no_such_mod.g at <unicode string>:1:5: no key in scope, no structural path and no importab
missing attr         -> ReferenceResolutionError | located: True | Cannot resolve !ref:os.path.no_such_attr at <unicode string>:1:5: no key in scope, no structural path and no i
${ref} syntax error  -> SyntaxError | located: False | invalid syntax (broken_mod.py, line 1)
```
**Expected:** a located `ReferenceResolutionError` (or `ConfigurationError`) that names the reference's line and carries the import failure as its cause — `AGENTS.md` → Typed exceptions: "Every error confluid raises MUST come from `confluid/exceptions.py` … never a bare builtin"; `docs/lifecycle.md` pass 2 already treats "a syntax error" as an import failure to report, not to re-raise raw. The `missing module` row shows the machinery exists for the `ImportError` case alone.
**Known?** NEW (R11 is the sibling for `_target_:` resolution; this is the reference import path)

## SR15 · S4 — A STRUCTURAL miss (index out of range, unknown bracket name, a key on a list, a trailing dot) is refused with the "reads the ATTRIBUTE … of the object built at …" message, naming an object and a rewrite that do not apply

**Where:** `confluid/resolver.py:367-379` — `refuse_attribute_reference` raises the attribute message for ANY first-key-in-context walk that returns `PATH_MISS`, whatever the reason; `:373` computes `attr = target.rsplit(".", 1)[-1]`, which for `items[5]` / `proto.` is the bracket text / the empty string.

**Repro:**

```python
from confluid import load, configurable
@configurable
class S:
    def __init__(self, v=1): self.v = v
for label, doc in [
    ("trailing dot        ", "proto: !class:S\nuse: !ref:proto.\n"),
    ("index out of range  ", "items: [x, y]\nuse: !ref:items[5]\n"),
    ("bracket str key     ", "names: {a: one}\nuse: !ref:names[a]\n"),
    ("key on a list       ", "items: [x, y]\nuse: !ref:items.b\n"),
    ("neg index (control) ", "items: [x, y]\nuse: !ref:items[-1]\n"),
]:
    try: print(label, "->", load(doc, until="settled"))
    except Exception as e: print(label, "->", type(e).__name__, str(e)[:130])
```
**Actual output:**
```
trailing dot         -> ConfigurationError !ref:proto. at <unicode string>:2:6 reads the ATTRIBUTE `` of the object built at `proto` — attribute references were removed (202
index out of range   -> ConfigurationError !ref:items[5] at <unicode string>:2:6 reads the ATTRIBUTE `items[5]` of the object built at `items` — attribute references were re
bracket str key      -> ConfigurationError !ref:names[a] at <unicode string>:2:6 reads the ATTRIBUTE `names[a]` of the object built at `names` — attribute references were re
key on a list        -> ConfigurationError !ref:items.b at <unicode string>:2:6 reads the ATTRIBUTE `b` of the object built at `items` — attribute references were removed (2
neg index (control)  -> {'items': ['x', 'y'], 'use': 'y'}
```
**Expected:** a refusal is right (the ruling), but one that names the cause — "index 5 out of range for `items` (2 entries)", "no key `a` in scope for `names[a]`" — `docs/targets.md`: the attribute refusal is for "Reading an attribute of a built object (`!ref:my_split.train`)", and `items` / `names` are plain containers that build nothing; `AGENTS.md` → "Concrete Examples": "An error message a user will read is an explanation". The suggested rewrite ("give `items`'s class a selector parameter") cannot be followed.
**Known?** NEW

## SR16 · S4 — A reference THROUGH a reference to a container (`alias: !ref:items` then `!ref:alias[1]` / `!ref:alias.k`) is refused as an attribute reference in every spelling

**Where:** `confluid/resolver.py:242-246` / `:247-264` — the structural walker steps `key` into a `dict` and `idx` into a `list`/`tuple`; the value at `alias` is a `Reference` object (pass 6 leaves a reference to a PLAIN container late-bound — `:567-569`), so the step misses, and `confluid/engine.py:590` refuses it before `:591-598` could chase the reference.

**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import load
for doc in ("items: [x, y]\nalias: !ref:items\nuse: !ref:alias[1]\n",
            "items: [x, y]\nalias: ${ref:items}\nuse: ${ref:alias[1]}\n",
            "d: {k: 1}\nalias: !ref:d\nuse: !ref:alias.k\n"):
    try: print(repr(doc[-30:]), "->", load(doc, until="settled"))
    except Exception as e: print(repr(doc[-30:]), "->", type(e).__name__, str(e)[:100])
```
**Actual output:**
```
'!ref:items\nuse: !ref:alias[1]\n' -> ConfigurationError !ref:alias[1] at <unicode string>:3:6 reads the ATTRIBUTE `alias[1]` of the object built at `alias` 
'f:items}\nuse: ${ref:alias[1]}\n' -> ConfigurationError !ref:alias[1] reads the ATTRIBUTE `alias[1]` of the object built at `alias` — attribute references w
'ias: !ref:d\nuse: !ref:alias.k\n' -> ConfigurationError !ref:alias.k at <unicode string>:3:6 reads the ATTRIBUTE `k` of the object built at `alias` — attrib
```
**Expected:** `use: 'y'` / `use: 1` — `docs/targets.md`: "a marker reached directly or through any number of `!ref:` resolves to one live instance" and a dotted reference "walks structure only — dict keys and list indices"; `alias` is a document key whose settled value IS a list. (A reference to a MARKER through a reference works, because pass 6 inlines marker-valued references — `a: !ref:b`, `b: !ref:c`, `c: !class:…` gives `a is c`; only container-valued chains fail.)
**Known?** NEW

## SR17 · S4 — `flow(Reference)` (the runtime `_flow_reference` path) disagrees with pass 7 on the same targets: a dotted path to a legal `null` is "Cannot resolve", and a reference to a LIST of markers is returned UNBUILT

**Where:** `confluid/engine.py:1586-1588` — `_flow_reference` falls back to `Resolver(...)._resolve_ref(obj.target)`, which keeps the legacy None-means-miss contract (`resolver.py:659-666`), so a found `None` is a miss; `:1579-1580` — `return flow(context[obj.target], …)` hands a list to `flow`, whose pass-through at `:668-670` returns any non-marker unchanged.

**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import load, flow, configurable
from confluid.fluid import Reference
from confluid.state import active_context
@configurable
class S:
    def __init__(self, v=1): self.v = v
doc = load("cfg: {x: null, y: 0}\nitems:\n  - !class:S\n    v: 1\n", until="document")
with active_context(doc):
    for t in ("cfg.x", "cfg.y", "items"):
        try: print(f"flow(Reference({t!r})) ->", repr(flow(Reference(t)))[:60])
        except Exception as e: print(f"flow(Reference({t!r})) ->", type(e).__name__, str(e)[:80])
print("pass 7 cfg.x :", load("cfg: {x: null}\nu: !ref:cfg.x\n")["u"])
print("pass 7 items :", load("items:\n  - !class:S\n    v: 1\nuse: !ref:items\n")["use"])
```
**Actual output:**
```
flow(Reference('cfg.x')) -> ReferenceResolutionError Cannot resolve Reference: cfg.x
flow(Reference('cfg.y')) -> 0
flow(Reference('items')) -> [Target(S, {'v': 1})]
pass 7 cfg.x : None
pass 7 items : [<__main__.S object at 0x10a8565a0>]
```
**Expected:** `None` and a list holding a built `S`, as pass 7 answers — `AGENTS.md` → "ONE path grammar, ONE policy": "The walker's MISS is the `PATH_MISS` sentinel, never `None` … a walk that FOUND a legal `null` is a hit" (SR6 fixed three consumers and left this fourth on the None contract); `_flow_reference`'s own docstring: "The exact whole-object key flows the referenced value". Blast radius: code-built `Reference`s flowed outside a load (a body-slot marker's kwargs, `flow(Reference(...))` from a consumer).
**Known?** NEW

---

### Suspected, not reproduced — SR

- **`!ref:` inside a `!partial:` builds a SECOND instance when the partial is flowed after the pass** (`opt: !partial:Opt {model: !ref:model}`; `flow(o["opt"]).model is o["model"]` → `False`, and two partials get two models). Reproduced (work/p18), NOT filed: `docs/targets.md` scopes identity to "Within one `load()` pass", and a later `flow()` is outside it. Worth a ruling — the pattern is the docs' own textbook case one spelling over.
- **`load(doc, scopes="debug")` (a `str` instead of a list)** iterates the characters and activates boolean scopes `d`, `e`, `b`, `u`, `g` silently (work/p29). Reproduced, not filed: a Python API type error (`Optional[List[str]]`), not a document.
- **`!ref:.proto` (leading dot) silently resolves as `proto`** — `_PATH_TOKEN_RE` allows an optional leading dot per token. Reproduced (work/p25), harmless, not filed.
- **`scopes._DECLARATION_LOCS` is a module global** cleared per check — two concurrent `load()`s could interleave declaration locations in one error message. Not reproduced (message-only).
- **`_settle_reference` prepares the referent in the REFERRER's view** (the `parent_context` handed to `_flow_recursive` at `engine.py:584`) — ENG-6 / BC3, OPEN in BUGS-2026-08-19; not re-probed.
- **A reference cycle (`a: !ref:b` / `b: !ref:a`) dies with a raw `RecursionError`** — SR5 (BUGS-2026-08-19), OPEN; `fold_reference_kwargs.referent_of` additionally drops the kwargs silently on such a chain (its `seen` guard returns `None`) before pass 7 recurses. Not refiled.
- **`!ref:items[idx]` with `idx: null`** is an `idxref` miss and gets the SR15 attribute message; not separately filed.
- **`_splice_key`'s `include` special case vs the `ScopeBlock` merge arm** — a block restating `include` where the existing value came from a block with the same dims merges two lists correctly; a nested block restating `include` inside a `ScopeBlock` that `deep_merge` merged (PA4 arm) was not probed.

# B· broadcast — pass 7 (broadcast.py + the pass-7 half of engine.py)
## BC1 · S1 — A class/instance block (or `Trainer.sched.opt.lr`) tuning a GRANDCHILD, written after a bare key, loses to the bare key — the C2 narrowing is one level deep while `tune_marker` recurses
**Where:** `confluid/engine.py:478-480` — `narrowed = _View(child_ctx)` / `for bk in lost: narrowed.pop(bk, None)` (`_view_for` narrows the view for the ADDRESSED slot only) and `:494` — `descend_context=child_ctx if k in beaten else None` (the grandchild's view is rebuilt from the UN-narrowed context); `confluid/broadcast.py:757` — `merged[key] = tune_marker(held, value) if isinstance(held, Target) and isinstance(value, dict) else value` (the block's value lands in the grandchild's OWN kwargs, i.e. at the grandchild's earlier position, with no verdict of its own); `confluid/broadcast.py:1544` — `return frozenset(k for k, i in _positions.items() if i < pos)` (`_bare_before()` is computed in the receiver's view — for a same-class block re-matched one level down that view is already narrowed, so the verdict comes out EMPTY and the grandchild sees the bare key again).
**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import configurable, load, configure, collect_report

@configurable
class Opt:
    def __init__(self, lr: float = -1.0, name: str = "opt"):
        self.lr, self.name = lr, name

@configurable
class Sched:
    def __init__(self, lr: float = -1.0, name: str = "sched", opt=None):
        self.lr, self.name, self.opt = lr, name, opt

@configurable
class Trainer:
    def __init__(self, lr: float = -1.0, name: str = "tr", sched=None):
        self.lr, self.name, self.sched = lr, name, sched

TREE = "t: !class:Trainer\n  lr: 0\n  sched: !class:Sched\n    lr: 0\n    opt: !class:Opt {lr: 0}\n"
def vals(r): t = r["t"]; return (t.lr, t.sched.lr, t.sched.opt.lr)
for tag, over in (
    ("block alone            ", "Trainer: {sched: {opt: {lr: 5}}}\n"),
    ("bare alone             ", "lr: 9\n"),
    ("bare THEN block        ", "lr: 9\nTrainer: {sched: {opt: {lr: 5}}}\n"),
    ("bare THEN 2-level block", "lr: 9\nSched: {opt: {lr: 5}}\n"),
    ("bare THEN instance blk ", "lr: 9\ntr: {sched: {opt: {lr: 5}}}\n"),
    ("bare THEN Trainer.sched.opt.lr", "lr: 9\nTrainer.sched.opt.lr: 5\n"),
):
    with collect_report() as rep:
        got = vals(load(TREE + over))
    live = Trainer(lr=0, sched=Sched(lr=0, opt=Opt(lr=0))); configure(config=over, t=live)
    print(f"{tag} load (t, sched, opt).lr = {got}   configure = {(live.lr, live.sched.lr, live.sched.opt.lr)}")
    if "THEN" in tag and "2-level" not in tag:
        print("    explain(lr, Opt):", rep.explain("lr", target="Opt 'opt'").replace("\n", " // "))
        print("    explain(lr, Sched):", rep.explain("lr", target="Sched 'sched'").replace("\n", " // "))
```
**Actual output:**
```
block alone             load (t, sched, opt).lr = (0, 0, 5)   configure = (0, 0, 5)
bare alone              load (t, sched, opt).lr = (9, 9, 9)   configure = (9, 9, 9)
bare THEN block         load (t, sched, opt).lr = (9, 0, 9)   configure = (9, 0, 9)
    explain(lr, Opt): lr on Opt 'opt' = 9 //     #0   own                5            beaten — earlier in the document //   ✓ #1   bare               9            applied
    explain(lr, Sched): 'lr' overrode nothing on "Sched 'sched'" — it is either unset, or set by a source that is not an override (the marker's own kwarg, or the constructor default). Overridden keys: lr, sched
bare THEN 2-level block load (t, sched, opt).lr = (9, 9, 5)   configure = (9, 9, 5)
bare THEN instance blk  load (t, sched, opt).lr = (9, 0, 9)   configure = (9, 0, 9)
    explain(lr, Opt): lr on Opt 'opt' = 9 //     #0   own                5            beaten — earlier in the document //   ✓ #1   bare               9            applied
    explain(lr, Sched): 'lr' overrode nothing on "Sched 'sched'" — it is either unset, or set by a source that is not an override (the marker's own kwarg, or the constructor default). Overridden keys: lr, sched
bare THEN Trainer.sched.opt.lr load (t, sched, opt).lr = (9, 0, 9)   configure = (9, 0, 9)
    explain(lr, Opt): lr on Opt 'opt' = 9 //     #0   own                5            beaten — earlier in the document //   ✓ #1   bare               9            applied
    explain(lr, Sched): 'lr' overrode nothing on "Sched 'sched'" — it is either unset, or set by a source that is not an override (the marker's own kwarg, or the constructor default). Overridden keys: lr, sched
```
The same-class-block shape (`hunt/BC/work/r02b_same_class_block.py`, a `Node` tree three deep, `Node: {child: {lr: 5}}` matches every level) shows the second face — the verdict computed inside a narrowed view is empty:
```
Node: {child: {lr: 5}}           load (a, a.child, a.child.child).lr = (0, 5, 5)   configure = (0, 5, 5)
lr: 9 | Node: {child: {lr: 5}}   load (a, a.child, a.child.child).lr = (9, 5, 9)   configure = (9, 5, 9)
```
**Expected:** `(9, 9, 5)` for the three-level rows and `(9, 5, 5)` for the `Node` row — the block is written AFTER the bare key, in the docs' own recommended layout ("Put document-wide defaults at the TOP and overrides below them"). `docs/broadcasting.md`: "`trainer: {lr: 0.001}` — identical to the dotted form — one rule, two spellings"; "There are **no specificity tiers**: an exact `trainer.lr` earlier in the document loses to a later `**.lr`, and vice versa." The one-level block (`Sched: {opt: {lr: 5}}`) gives 5; the two-level one gives 9 — two spellings of one delivery disagree, and `explain` labels the block's value "own" at #0 so the losing block is invisible as a block. (The `sched.lr = 0` in the same rows — the bare key cut off from a node whose slot the block tuned without mentioning `lr` — is what AGENTS' C2 text calls "the narrowed view protects that marker's own kwargs"; not filed separately, but it is the same per-slot-not-per-key verdict as BC6.)
**Known?** NEW — BUGS-2026-08-19 BC5 (FIXED) pinned the opposite direction (a grandchild the block did NOT address must still see the bare key); the grandchild the block DID address was not pinned.

## BC3 · S2 — A plain key that re-declares a head an EARLIER dotted line created keeps the dotted line's position, so adding an earlier, losing line flips the later contest (the marker written LAST loses to a bare key written before it)
**Where:** `confluid/merger.py:284` — `result[key] = copy_value(value)` (the plain-key branch of `expand_dotted_mapping` assigns into a key the dotted head already created; Python keeps the existing key's position, so the marker inherits the dotted line's slot; `deep_merge` re-anchors an overlay key, this branch does not). Pass 5 — filed here because its face is a pass-7 ordering inversion; flagging for the merger reviewer.
**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import configurable, load, collect_report

@configurable
class Trainer:
    def __init__(self, lr: float = -1.0, name: str = "tr"):
        self.lr, self.name = lr, name

CONTROL = "lr: 2\nt: !class:Trainer {lr: 0}\n"                 # the marker is the LAST spec -> its own lr: 0 wins
ADDED   = "t.lr: 1\nlr: 2\nt: !class:Trainer {lr: 0}\n"         # one EARLIER, losing line added on top; nothing else changed
for tag, doc in (("control", CONTROL), ("+ earlier t.lr", ADDED)):
    with collect_report() as rep:
        r = load(doc)
    print(f"{tag:16s} t.lr = {r['t'].lr}   key order after expansion: {list(load(doc, until='document'))}")
    print(f"{'':16s} explain: {rep.explain('lr').replace(chr(10), ' // ')}")
```
**Actual output:**
```
control          t.lr = 0   key order after expansion: ['lr', 't']
                 explain: 'lr' overrode nothing — it is either unset, or set by a source that is not an override (the marker's own kwarg, or the constructor default). Overridden keys: (nothing was applied in this pass)
+ earlier t.lr   t.lr = 2   key order after expansion: ['t', 'lr']
                 explain: lr on Trainer 'tr' = 2 //     #0   own                0            beaten — earlier in the document //   ✓ #1   bare               2            applied
```
The pairwise matrix (`p01_matrix.py`) shows the same flip for every spelling between the dotted line and the marker: `t.lr: 1 | Trainer: {lr: 2} | t: …` → 2, `t.lr: 1 | '**': {lr: 2} | t: …` → 2, `t.lr: 1 | tr: {lr: 2} | t: …` → 2 (seven rows), expected 0 in all.
**Expected:** 0 in both — the marker is the latest spec. `AGENTS.md`: "`merger.deep_merge` re-anchors a key the overlay re-states at the OVERLAY's position, so a key written on both sides survives once, at the later position, with the later value"; the PA24 ruling says "A LATER marker REPLACES an earlier dotted head" — it replaces the VALUE here but inherits the earlier line's POSITION, which is the BC9 defect ("adding an EARLIER, losing root key flips which LATER spec wins") one producer over.
**Known?** NEW — BUGS-2026-08-19 BC9 (FIXED) repaired `_View.set`'s keep-the-position in the engine's grouping-dict path; the merger's plain-key branch has the same keep.

---
## BC4 · S2 — A glob block that reaches a node through a dotted line or a class/instance block (`t.**.lr`, `t.*.lr`, `Trainer: {'**': …}`, `Trainer: {'*': …}`) is spliced at the MARKER's slot, so written AFTER a bare key (or a class block) it loses to it for the descendants — and `explain` reports the rider at the marker's index
**Where:** `confluid/broadcast.py:1562` — `route("**", bv)` (a block-delivered rider, inside `_consume`), `:1581` — `route(bk, bv)` (a `'*'` block), `:1631` — `route("**", v)` (`_consume_own`, the dotted-folded rider) → `:1796` `_merge_routing(self.merged, key, block)` → `:777` `out.set(key, block, _KeyScope.BARE if key == "**" else _KeyScope.STRICT)` into `merged`, which `_splice_kwargs_at_slot` emits at `self_key`'s position (`:1004` `for kk, kv in kwargs.items(): … _emit_merged(kk, kv)` under `if k == self_key`) — the marker's position, never the line's or the block's. The BC4 stamp (`merger.py:327-339`) fires only when the dotted path ENDS inside the marker's kwargs; `t.**.lr` ends in the fresh `'**'` dict, where `owner` is reset to `None`.
**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import configurable, load, configure, collect_report

@configurable
class Opt:
    def __init__(self, lr: float = -1.0, name: str = "opt"):
        self.lr, self.name = lr, name

@configurable
class Trainer:
    def __init__(self, lr: float = -1.0, name: str = "tr", opt=None):
        self.lr, self.name, self.opt = lr, name, opt

TREE = "t: !class:Trainer\n  lr: 0\n  opt: !class:Opt {lr: 0}\n"
for tag, over in (
    ("t.**.lr AFTER bare      ", "lr: 9\nt.**.lr: 5\n"),
    ("t.*.lr AFTER bare       ", "lr: 9\nt.*.lr: 5\n"),
    ("Trainer{'**'} AFTER bare", "lr: 9\nTrainer: {'**': {lr: 5}}\n"),
    ("Trainer{'*'} AFTER bare ", "lr: 9\nTrainer: {'*': {lr: 5}}\n"),
    ("t.**.lr AFTER Opt block ", "Opt: {lr: 9}\nt.**.lr: 5\n"),
    ("control: '**' AFTER bare", "lr: 9\n'**': {lr: 5}\n"),
    ("control: t.**.lr BEFORE ", "t.**.lr: 5\nlr: 9\n"),
):
    with collect_report() as rep:
        r = load(TREE + over)
    live = Trainer(lr=0, opt=Opt(lr=0)); configure(config=over, t=live)
    print(f"{tag} load (t, opt).lr = {(r['t'].lr, r['t'].opt.lr)}   configure = {(live.lr, live.opt.lr)}")
    if "AFTER" in tag and "control" not in tag:
        print("    explain(lr, Opt):", rep.explain("lr", target="Opt 'opt'").replace("\n", " // "))
```
**Actual output:**
```
t.**.lr AFTER bare       load (t, opt).lr = (9, 9)   configure = (9, 9)
    explain(lr, Opt): lr on Opt 'opt' = 9 //     #0   own                0            beaten — earlier in the document //     #1   glob '**'          5            beaten — earlier in the document //   ✓ #2   bare               9            applied
t.*.lr AFTER bare        load (t, opt).lr = (9, 9)   configure = (9, 9)
    explain(lr, Opt): lr on Opt 'opt' = 9 //     #0   own                0            beaten — earlier in the document //     #1   glob '*'           5            beaten — earlier in the document //   ✓ #2   bare               9            applied
Trainer{'**'} AFTER bare load (t, opt).lr = (5, 9)   configure = (5, 9)
    explain(lr, Opt): lr on Opt 'opt' = 9 //     #0   own                0            beaten — earlier in the document //     #1   glob '**'          5            beaten — earlier in the document //   ✓ #2   bare               9            applied
Trainer{'*'} AFTER bare  load (t, opt).lr = (9, 9)   configure = (9, 9)
    explain(lr, Opt): lr on Opt 'opt' = 9 //     #0   own                0            beaten — earlier in the document //     #1   glob '*'           5            beaten — earlier in the document //   ✓ #2   bare               9            applied
t.**.lr AFTER Opt block  load (t, opt).lr = (5, 9)   configure = (5, 9)
    explain(lr, Opt): lr on Opt 'opt' = 9 //     #1   own                0            beaten — earlier in the document //     #2   glob '**'          5            beaten — earlier in the document //   ✓ #3   block 'Opt'        9            applied
control: '**' AFTER bare load (t, opt).lr = (5, 5)   configure = (5, 5)
control: t.**.lr BEFORE  load (t, opt).lr = (9, 9)   configure = (9, 9)
```
**Expected:** `(5, 5)` for `t.**.lr` / `Trainer: {'**': …}` after the bare key, `(9, 5)` for the `'*'` forms, `(5, 5)` for `t.**.lr` after `Opt: {lr: 9}` — the rider is the later line. `docs/broadcasting.md` table: "`trainer.**.lr: 0.001` — trainer **and** all its descendants"; "There are **no specificity tiers**: an exact `trainer.lr` earlier in the document loses to a later `**.lr`, and vice versa"; "All three spellings converge inside blocks too: `trainer: {'**.lr': 1}` ≡ `trainer: {'**': {lr: 1}}` ≡ `trainer.**.lr: 1`". The root rider (control row) orders correctly; the scoped one does not — two spellings of one delivery — and `explain` prints the rider at `#1` (the marker's index) although it was written at line 4. The `Trainer: {'**': …}` row delivers 5 to the Trainer itself (block position) and 9 to its child (marker position) from ONE block.
**Known?** NEW. Related to BUGS-2026-08-19 BC10 (DEFERRED: two riders collapse into one `'**'` key) but a different mechanism — no rider merge is involved; the hoisted block simply takes the marker's slot. If the ruling folds it into BC10's "per-key rider positions" deferral, the docs table row and the "converge inside blocks" sentence need the caveat.

---
## BC5 · S2 — Inside ONE class block (and its dotted twins) a `'**'` rider written BEFORE a slot mapping beats the mapping on that slot's node: the block's internal order is lost; the owner-dotted twin diverges across paths
**Where:** `confluid/broadcast.py:1781` — `self.merged.set(key, tune_marker(prev, block), _KeyScope.EXACT)` (`_View.set` keeps the slot's EARLIER position from the own-kwarg unroll, while the rider hoisted at `:1562` → `:777` is APPENDED to `merged` after it — so in the child view the rider sits AFTER the slot although it was written BEFORE the mapping). For the `t.**.lr: 9 | t.sched.lr: 5` row: `confluid/merger.py:339` — `stamps[last] = frozenset(k for k in result if isinstance(k, str))` names top-level keys only; a rider folded into the SAME marker has no top-level key, so `_dotted_protected(bk, via="**")` at `:1588` is never true.
**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import configurable, load, configure

@configurable
class Sched:
    def __init__(self, lr: float = -1.0, name: str = "sched"):
        self.lr, self.name = lr, name

@configurable
class Trainer:
    def __init__(self, lr: float = -1.0, name: str = "tr", sched=None):
        self.lr, self.name, self.sched = lr, name, sched

TREE = "t: !class:Trainer\n  lr: 0\n  sched: !class:Sched {lr: 0}\n"
for over in (
    "Trainer: {'**': {lr: 9}, sched: {lr: 5}}\n",     # rider FIRST, mapping SECOND -> the mapping is the later spec
    "Trainer: {sched: {lr: 5}, '**': {lr: 9}}\n",     # mapping FIRST, rider SECOND -> the rider is the later spec
    "Trainer.**.lr: 9\nTrainer.sched.lr: 5\n",        # the dotted twin of line 1
    "t.**.lr: 9\nt.sched.lr: 5\n",                    # the owner-dotted twin of line 1
    "t.sched.lr: 5\nt.**.lr: 9\n",                    # its reverse
):
    r = load(TREE + over)
    live = Trainer(lr=0, sched=Sched(lr=0)); configure(config=over, t=live)
    print(f"{over.strip().replace(chr(10), ' | '):44s} load: sched.lr = {r['t'].sched.lr}   configure: sched.lr = {live.sched.lr}")
```
**Actual output:**
```
Trainer: {'**': {lr: 9}, sched: {lr: 5}}     load: sched.lr = 9   configure: sched.lr = 9
Trainer: {sched: {lr: 5}, '**': {lr: 9}}     load: sched.lr = 9   configure: sched.lr = 9
Trainer.**.lr: 9 | Trainer.sched.lr: 5       load: sched.lr = 9   configure: sched.lr = 9
t.**.lr: 9 | t.sched.lr: 5                   load: sched.lr = 9   configure: sched.lr = 5
t.sched.lr: 5 | t.**.lr: 9                   load: sched.lr = 9   configure: sched.lr = 9
```
**Expected:** 5 in rows 1, 3 and 4 (the mapping is the later line), 9 in rows 2 and 5 — both orderings of one pair must DISAGREE. `docs/broadcasting.md`: "Class-name and instance-name dict blocks (`Foo: {...}`) are unrolled inline at their position" (`_prepare_kwargs` docstring) and "last write wins"; the marker's OWN block honours the order (`t: {'**': {lr: 9}, sched: !class:Sched {lr: 5}}` → 5, reversed → 9, measured in `p25_inblock_order.py`), so the class-block spelling of the same two lines answers differently from the own-kwarg spelling. Row 4 is also a load/configure divergence: `docs/configure.md` "Both paths report identically ... because both run the one scanner".
**Known?** NEW.

---
## BC6 · S2 — A bare key written BEFORE a slot mapping that does not mention it never reaches a code-born deferred slot: the CODE default survives a document key (and is reported `unused`), while the dotted spelling of the same mapping lets it land
**Where:** `confluid/broadcast.py:881` — `return {slot: frozenset(k for k, i in positions.items() if i > order[slot]) for slot in slots}` (`_late_bare_keys_per_slot`: every cascade key positioned BEFORE the slot is excluded, whether or not the mapping carries that key) → `confluid/engine.py:1315` — `pool = {bk: bv for bk, bv in (broadcast_ctx or {}).items() if bk in late}` and `:1329` — `tuned._order_resolved = True` (after which `_broadcast_onto_instance` → `merge_bare_pool_into_kwargs(protected=frozenset(broadcasted))` never re-offers `wd`). The class-block spelling takes the C2 verdict instead (`engine.py:527` — `late[slot] = frozenset(k for k in pool if k not in out_positioned)`), which is per-slot in the same way.
**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import configurable, load, configure, PartialClass, collect_report

@configurable
class Opt:
    def __init__(self, params=None, lr: float = -1.0, wd: float = -1.0):
        self.params, self.lr, self.wd = params, lr, wd

@configurable
class Trainer:
    def __init__(self, name: str = "tr"):
        self.name = name
        self.optimizer = PartialClass(Opt, lr=0, wd=0)   # the documented code-born deferred slot

for doc in (
    "wd: 9\nt: !class:Trainer\n",                               # control: the bare key alone overrides the code default
    "wd: 9\nt: !class:Trainer {optimizer: {lr: 5}}\n",          # a mapping that never mentions wd is written AFTER it
    "t: !class:Trainer\nwd: 9\nTrainer: {optimizer: {lr: 5}}\n",# class-block spelling of the same
    "t: !class:Trainer\nwd: 9\nt.optimizer.lr: 5\n",            # dotted spelling of the same
    "t: !class:Trainer {optimizer: {lr: 5}}\nwd: 9\n",          # control: wd AFTER the mapping
):
    with collect_report() as rep:
        kw = dict(load(doc)["t"].optimizer.kwargs)
    print(f"{doc.strip().replace(chr(10), ' | '):52s} optimizer.kwargs = {kw}   unused = {rep.unused}")
live = Trainer(); rep = configure(live, config="wd: 9\nTrainer: {optimizer: {lr: 5}}\n")
print(f"configure(Trainer(), 'wd: 9 | Trainer: {{optimizer: {{lr: 5}}}}')            optimizer.kwargs = {dict(live.optimizer.kwargs)}   unused = {rep.unused}")
```
**Actual output:**
```
wd: 9 | t: !class:Trainer                            optimizer.kwargs = {'lr': 0, 'wd': 9}   unused = []
wd: 9 | t: !class:Trainer {optimizer: {lr: 5}}       optimizer.kwargs = {'lr': 5, 'wd': 0}   unused = ['wd']
t: !class:Trainer | wd: 9 | Trainer: {optimizer: {lr: 5}} optimizer.kwargs = {'lr': 5, 'wd': 0}   unused = ['wd']
t: !class:Trainer | wd: 9 | t.optimizer.lr: 5        optimizer.kwargs = {'lr': 5, 'wd': 9}   unused = []
t: !class:Trainer {optimizer: {lr: 5}} | wd: 9       optimizer.kwargs = {'lr': 5, 'wd': 9}   unused = []
configure(Trainer(), 'wd: 9 | Trainer: {optimizer: {lr: 5}}')            optimizer.kwargs = {'lr': 5, 'wd': 0}   unused = ['wd']
```
**Expected:** `wd = 9` in every row — `wd: 9` is a document key and `wd=0` is a code default with no position; the mapping never mentions `wd`, so it is not a later spec FOR `wd`. `docs/broadcasting.md`: "A value set in *code* — `self.optimizer = PartialClass(AdamW, lr=1e-4)` — has no position in the document at all, so it is a default: any `lr:` overrides it, exactly as it would override a constructor default"; "**A block merges, it does not replace.** `optimizer: {lr: 0.5}` keeps every kwarg you did not mention" (keeps the kwarg — it does not promote a code default ahead of a document key). The mapping and dotted spellings of one override disagree on an unrelated sibling key. Scope note: the verdict machinery already exists; this asks that the per-slot set be intersected with the mapping's keys. If the ruling is that a mapping re-declares the whole slot at its position, the two doc sentences above need that caveat and the `unused` record is the only warning a reader gets.
**Known?** NEW.

## BC7 · S2 — A dict value in a class/instance block for a `**kwargs` target is silently dropped — routed as a sub-block that never lands, no record — while the own-kwarg and dotted spellings deliver it to the constructor
**Where:** `confluid/broadcast.py:1400` — `if param_kinds.get(k) == "dict": return acceptable is None or k in acceptable` / `return False` (`_accepts`: a dict is a VALUE only for a dict-annotated param; a `**kwargs` target has no param kinds, so never) → `:1419` — `if acceptable is None or k not in acceptable: return False` (`_dict_slot`: a `**kwargs` target has no declared slot either) → `:1580-1581` — `if not floating: route(bk, bv)` (hoisted as STRICT routing for direct children the class has none of; no `unknown()`, no report record).
**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import configurable, load, configure, collect_report

@configurable
class Sink:
    def __init__(self, name: str = "sink", **kwargs):
        self.name = name
        self.options = dict(kwargs)

for doc in (
    "s: !class:Sink {table: {a: 1}}\n",          # own kwarg
    "s: !class:Sink\ns.table: {a: 1}\n",         # dotted
    "s: !class:Sink\nSink: {table: {a: 1}}\n",   # class block
    "s: !class:Sink\nsink: {table: {a: 1}}\n",   # instance block
):
    with collect_report() as rep:
        s = load(doc)["s"]
    print(f"{doc.strip().replace(chr(10), ' | '):36s} ctor kwargs = {s.options}  attr table = {getattr(s, 'table', '<absent>')}  applied={[(a.key, a.origin) for a in rep.applied]} failed={rep.failed} unused={rep.unused}")
live = Sink(); rep = configure(live, config="Sink: {table: {a: 1}}\n")
print(f"configure(Sink(), 'Sink: {{table: {{a: 1}}}}')    ctor kwargs = {live.options}  attr table = {getattr(live, 'table', '<absent>')}  applied={rep.applied} failed={rep.failed} unused={rep.unused}")
```
**Actual output:**
```
s: !class:Sink {table: {a: 1}}       ctor kwargs = {'table': {'a': 1}}  attr table = <absent>  applied=[] failed=[] unused=[]
s: !class:Sink | s.table: {a: 1}     ctor kwargs = {'table': {'a': 1}}  attr table = <absent>  applied=[] failed=[] unused=[]
s: !class:Sink | Sink: {table: {a: 1}} ctor kwargs = {}  attr table = <absent>  applied=[] failed=[] unused=[]
s: !class:Sink | sink: {table: {a: 1}} ctor kwargs = {}  attr table = <absent>  applied=[] failed=[] unused=[]
configure(Sink(), 'Sink: {table: {a: 1}}')    ctor kwargs = {}  attr table = <absent>  applied=[] failed=[] unused=[]
```
**Expected:** `ctor kwargs = {'table': {'a': 1}}` in every row — `docs/broadcasting.md`, the `**kwargs` table: "written on the marker (`!class:Passthrough(tag=x)`), **or in a block naming it** → the **constructor** (`kwargs`)". The block spelling delivers nothing and says nothing (`applied`/`failed`/`unused` all empty). BUGS-2026-08-19 BC15 (marker) and BC16 (list) fixed the sibling shapes on 2026-08-20; the dict shape was left.
**Known?** NEW.

## BC8 · S4 — A bare key or `'**'` rider beaten by a LATER dotted line is reported `unused` and is missing from `explain`'s contest, while the same key beaten by a later class block / rider / own kwarg is `used` and listed
**Where:** `confluid/broadcast.py:1709` — `if blocked is not None and k not in blocked and accepts_value(k, v) and not _dotted_protected(k, k): apply(k, v, "bare", _KeyScope.BARE, False, True, pos)` (the BC4 skip happens BEFORE `apply`, so neither `_MergeSink._mark_used` nor the contest ledger `self.contest.setdefault(key, []).append((origin, value, pos))` at `:1760` ever sees the beaten candidate); `:1588` — `and not _dotted_protected(bk, via)` does the same for block/rider deliveries.
**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import configurable, load, collect_report

@configurable
class Trainer:
    def __init__(self, lr: float = -1.0, name: str = "tr"):
        self.lr, self.name = lr, name

for doc in (
    "t: !class:Trainer {lr: 0}\nlr: 5\nTrainer: {lr: 9}\n",   # bare beaten by a later class block
    "t: !class:Trainer {lr: 0}\nlr: 5\n'**': {lr: 9}\n",      # bare beaten by a later rider
    "t: !class:Trainer {lr: 0}\nlr: 5\nt.lr: 9\n",            # bare beaten by a later DOTTED line
    "t: !class:Trainer {lr: 0}\n'**': {lr: 5}\nt.lr: 9\n",    # rider beaten by a later DOTTED line
):
    with collect_report() as rep:
        r = load(doc)
    print(f"{doc.strip().replace(chr(10), ' | '):48s} t.lr = {r['t'].lr}  unused = {rep.unused}  applied = {[(a.key, a.origin) for a in rep.applied]}")
    print(f"{'':48s} explain: {rep.explain('lr').replace(chr(10), ' // ')}")
```
**Actual output:**
```
t: !class:Trainer {lr: 0} | lr: 5 | Trainer: {lr: 9} t.lr = 9  unused = []  applied = [('lr', "block 'Trainer'")]
                                                 explain: lr on Trainer 'tr' = 9 //     #0   own                0            beaten — earlier in the document //     #1   bare               5            beaten — earlier in the document //   ✓ #2   block 'Trainer'    9            applied
t: !class:Trainer {lr: 0} | lr: 5 | '**': {lr: 9} t.lr = 9  unused = []  applied = [('lr', "glob '**'")]
                                                 explain: lr on Trainer 'tr' = 9 //     #0   own                0            beaten — earlier in the document //     #1   bare               5            beaten — earlier in the document //   ✓ #2   glob '**'          9            applied
t: !class:Trainer {lr: 0} | lr: 5 | t.lr: 9      t.lr = 9  unused = ['lr']  applied = []
                                                 explain: 'lr' overrode nothing — it is either unset, or set by a source that is not an override (the marker's own kwarg, or the constructor default). Overridden keys: (nothing was applied in this pass)
t: !class:Trainer {lr: 0} | '**': {lr: 5} | t.lr: 9 t.lr = 9  unused = ['**.lr']  applied = []
                                                 explain: 'lr' overrode nothing — it is either unset, or set by a source that is not an override (the marker's own kwarg, or the constructor default). Overridden keys: (nothing was applied in this pass)
```
**Expected:** `unused = []` in rows 3–4 (the key matched Trainer and lost — `docs/report.md`: "`report.unused` — top-level document keys that matched **nothing** across the whole pass"), and a contest listing own 0 / bare 5 / dotted 9 with the dotted line marked — `docs/report.md`: "`explain` ... prints the contest in document order with the winner marked"; `AGENTS.md`: "`_MergeSink.apply` records BEFORE its own-kwarg early return. A marker's own kwarg is a competitor like any other". Rows 1–2 and rows 3–4 are the same contest with a different winner's spelling and report opposite `unused` verdicts. (Whether the dotted winner itself should have a line is the documented "own kwarg has no line" limit; the missing LOSERS are not.)
**Known?** NEW (a reporting face of the BUGS-2026-08-19 BC4 fix).

## Re-hit, already open or ruled — NOT refiled

- **BUGS-2026-08-19 BC7 (open):** `Trainer: {opt: {lr: 7}}` at a slot holding `!ref:shared` still replaces the reference with the raw dict — `p09_ref_twice.py` row "S A B, Trainer {opt: {lr}}": `AttributeError: 'dict' object has no attribute 'lr'`.
- **BUGS-2026-08-19 BC3 (open):** `a.**.lr: 7` on a referrer of a ROOT-defined `shared` leaves `shared.lr = 0` when `shared` is written first (its row 1); not re-derived further.
- **BC10 (DEFERRED) family, merger face:** two ROOT riders (`'**': {lr: 1}` then `'**.lr': 2`, or `'*'`/`'*.lr'`) expand into ONE key at the FIRST's position, so `'**': {lr: 1} | t: !class:Trainer {lr: 0} | '**.lr': 2` → `t.lr = 0` (`p01_matrix.py` rows 8–11; key order after expansion `['t', '**']`). Same one-position-per-`'**'`-key limit as BC10, one pass earlier.
- **BC11 (DEFERRED):** `h.*.lr`, `h.**.lr`, `Strict: {…}`, `Pinned: {…}`, `s: {…}`, `p: {…}` reach a code-born `PartialClass` body slot under `configure()` and not under `load()` (`p11_strict_nobroadcast.py`).
- **BC13 ruling (F11's move stays):** an instance-name block equal to the attribute key (`opt: {lr: 9}` with `name: opt`) written BEFORE the marker still wins — `(0, 9)` instead of `(0, 0)` (`p13_instname_attrkey.py` row 2); the displacement makes the block the later spec regardless of its line, which the 2026-08-20 ruling kept as is.
- **CD21 (documented):** a constructor-built child + an own mapping hands the dict to the constructor (first run of `p22_unmentioned_key.py`); the probe was rewritten with a body slot.

### Suspected, not reproduced — BC

- `_View.__delitem__` / `clear()` leave the scope side-table stale (`p16_view.py`: after `del u["b"]` → `scopes` still carries `b`; after `clear()` → both tags remain) — no engine call site uses `del`/`clear` on a view (grep over `engine.py`/`broadcast.py`/`configurator.py`: none), so unreachable through the public surface; latent only.
- `configure()`'s `strict_attrs` refusal carries no `file:line` (`Strict has no attribute 'ghost' and is declared strict_attrs=True …`) while the load path's does (`… 'ghost' at <unicode string>:3:6 …`) — a live object has no file; the message names class and key. Listed, not filed.
- An instance name delivered by a bare key, a class block or a rider (`name: foo` then `foo: {lr: 9}`) is not matched by the instance block on EITHER path (`p23_name_delivered.py`: `lr = 0`, `unused = ['foo']`); consistent across paths and matches the documented rule ("the marker's `name` kwarg, falling back to the class's ctor default").
- An unconsumed glob leaf INSIDE a matched class block (`Trainer: {'**': {ghost: 1}}`, `t.*.ghost: 9`) appears in neither `failed` nor `unused` (`p10_explain_report.py`) — by the letter of `docs/report.md` (top-level keys register; a matched block is used).
- `_record_named_overlay` writes an `applied`/`addressed` record for an overlay value that a later bare key beats (`configure({t: {lr: 5}, lr: 9}, t=)` → both `('lr', "Trainer 't'", 'addressed')` and `('lr', "Trainer 'tr'", 'bare')`, `explain(target="Trainer 't'")` → "nothing else competed for it") — written by the AGENTS rule "each key the overlay hands the object … is ONE applied record"; noted as a consequence of BC2's fold, not filed on its own.
- Same-class markers under gc pressure: 400 siblings, `gc.set_threshold(1, 1, 1)`, eight tune paths (class block one/two levels, instance blocks, per-node dotted, bare, rider, body-slot tune) on `load()` and `configure()` — 0 cross-wired children, 400/400 distinct at every level (`p14_gc_pressure.py`).
- Threads: 6 threads × 25 × (`load()` of a 60-node document + `configure()`) with `sys.setswitchinterval(1e-6)` — 0 errors, no cross-contamination, no `KeyError` from the cache clears (`p15_threads.py`).
- Per-pass caches after a same-name class redefinition (NoBroadcast added, a param added) between two `load()`s and before a `configure()`, and two same-qualname classes alive in ONE document — every answer fresh (`p17_cache_stale.py`).
- `_View(v)`, `.copy()`, `.update()` from a `_View` / plain dict / pair list / iterator / kwargs, `copy.copy`, `copy.deepcopy` — tags preserved or cleared exactly as the docstring says; `beaten_per_slot` is not inherited (`p16_view.py`).
- Every other cell of the matrices agrees with document order on both paths: 157/168 pairwise cells (the 11 misses are BC3 ×7 and the BC10-family merge ×4), 26/30 nested cells (BC1 ×2, BC2 ×2), 14/16 deferred cells (BC2 ×2), 14/15 list-kwarg cells (BC4 ×1), 12/12 `!ref:`-twice layouts bar the open BC7/BC3, 12/12 instance-name layouts bar the ruled F11 row, 13/13 wrapper-shield rows that the BC12 ruling covers (the own-kwarg shield blocks an earlier sweep; the `Wrapper: {lr: 5}` class-block and `w.lr: 5` dotted spellings of a shield do not block an earlier bare key — `p20_shield_pairs.py` rows "bare, Wrapper-blk shield" → 9 and "bare, w.lr dotted shield" → 9 — the block form is dropped as `unknown-attribute` and the dotted form's BC4 stamp is not consulted by `_splice_kwargs_at_slot`; listed here rather than filed because the shield idiom is documented for "a kwarg set on a wrapper block" and the pins use the own-kwarg spelling only — a ruling on whether the other two spellings are shields is needed before this is a bug).

# E· engine — flow / instantiate / solidify (passes 8–9, state.py)
## ENG-1 · S1 — A mapping addressed at a `Partial[T]` CONSTRUCTOR param whose default is a `PartialClass(...)` is passed to the constructor as a plain dict: the deferred marker is replaced (warn/off) or construction fails with a misleading error (strict) — in every spelling
**Where:** `confluid/engine.py:776` — `ctor = dict(merged) if params is _UNKNOWN_PARAMS else {k: v for k, v in merged.items() if k in params}` — `opt` IS a constructor parameter, so the mapping `{lr: 0.5}` goes into the constructor call as the `opt` argument; the tune arm `confluid/engine.py:1297` — `elif isinstance(v, dict) and isinstance(existing, Target):` — is reachable only for keys that are NOT in `ctor` (`:1237` `if k not in ctor:`), and pass 7's `dict_at_slot` cannot see a ctor DEFAULT marker (it is not in the document). Nothing tunes.
**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import configurable, load, PartialClass, Partial, set_policy
class Opt:
    def __init__(self, params=None, lr: float = 0.1, wd: float = 0.0): self.lr, self.wd = lr, wd
@configurable
class CtorHost:                       # the canonical spelling from confluid/partial.py's docstring + docs/targets.md
    def __init__(self, opt: Partial[Opt] = PartialClass(Opt, lr=0.1, wd=0.01)): self.opt = opt
@configurable
class BodyHost:                       # the body-slot spelling of the same slot, for contrast
    def __init__(self): self.opt: Partial[Opt] = PartialClass(Opt, lr=0.1, wd=0.01)
for mode in ("strict", "warn"):
    set_policy(yaml=mode)
    for cls in ("CtorHost", "BodyHost"):
        doc = f"h: {{_target_: {cls}, opt: {{lr: 0.5}}}}"        # `h.opt.lr: 0.5` and a `{cls}: {{opt: {{lr: 0.5}}}}` block behave identically
        try: print(f"{mode:6} {cls:8} -> h.opt = {load(doc)['h'].opt!r}")
        except Exception as e: print(f"{mode:6} {cls:8} -> {type(e).__name__}: {str(e).splitlines()[0][:80]} ...")
```
**Actual output:**
```
2026-08-22 05:19:26 | WARNING  | confluid.validation:validate_kwargs:300 | CtorHost: invalid configuration at <unicode string>:1:4
4 validation errors for CtorHostConfig
opt.is-instance[Opt]
  Input should be an instance of Opt [type=is_instance_of, input_value={'lr': 0.5}, input_type=dict]
    For further information visit https://errors.pydantic.dev/2.13/v/is_instance_of
opt.is-instance[Fluid]
  Input should be an instance of Fluid [type=is_instance_of, input_value={'lr': 0.5}, input_type=dict]
    For further information visit https://errors.pydantic.dev/2.13/v/is_instance_of

  Field required [type=missing, input_value={'lr': 0.5}, input_type=dict]
    For further information visit https://errors.pydantic.dev/2.13/v/missing
opt.FluidConfig.lr
  Extra inputs are not permitted [type=extra_forbidden, input_value=0.5, input_type=float]
    For further information visit https://errors.pydantic.dev/2.13/v/extra_forbidden
strict CtorHost -> ConstructionError: Failed to construct CtorHost at <unicode string>:1:4: 4 validation errors for Ct ...

warn   CtorHost -> h.opt = {'lr': 0.5}
```

(The dotted spelling `h.opt.lr: 0.5` and the class block `CtorHost: {opt: {lr: 0.5}}` were measured too — identical results per mode; see `work/p99b_ctor_default_partial_tune.py`.)
**Expected:** the mapping tunes the deferred default — `PartialClass(Opt, {'lr': 0.5, 'wd': 0.01})`, exactly what the body-slot spelling gives — `docs/targets.md`: "A mapping addressed at a slot already holding a deferred marker TUNES it, never replaces it." and `docs/architecture.md` record 5: "a mapping addressed at its slot tunes it rather than replacing it … (Before the tuning half, the mapping *replaced* the slot: `optimizer: {lr: 0.5}` left a plain `dict` where an optimizer belonged, and the kwargs set in code — `weight_decay` — vanished with it.)" — which is precisely the warn-mode output above, on the spelling `confluid/partial.py` and `docs/targets.md` ("Python-side `Partial[T]` annotation") both present as canonical.
**Known?** NEW

## ENG-2 · S2 — Markers inside a CONTAINER-valued `__init__`-body slot are neither built nor broadcast into: `self.engines = [Target(Engine)]` stays an unbuilt list of markers, `self.optimizers = [PartialClass(Opt, lr=…)]` ignores a bare `lr:`
**Where:** `confluid/engine.py:1470-1471` — `if not isinstance(attr_val, Fluid): continue` — the post-construction sweep visits only attributes that ARE a Fluid; a list / dict holding markers is skipped, while `_resolve_kwarg_value` (`:998-1007`) does recurse containers for constructor kwargs.
**Repro:**
```python
from typing import List
from confluid import configurable, PartialClass, Partial, load, collect_report, Target
class Opt:
    def __init__(self, params=None, lr: float = 0.1): self.params, self.lr = params, lr
@configurable
class Engine:
    def __init__(self, power: int = 1): self.power = power
@configurable
class Trainer:
    def __init__(self):
        self.optimizers: List[Partial[Opt]] = [PartialClass(Opt, lr=0.1)]   # deferred markers in a LIST body slot
        self.engines = [Target(Engine)]                                      # eager markers in a LIST body slot
        self.engine = Target(Engine)                                         # the direct spelling, for contrast
with collect_report() as rep:
    t = load("t: {_target_: Trainer}\nlr: 0.7\npower: 9\n")["t"]
print("optimizers:", t.optimizers)
print("engines   :", t.engines)
print("engine    :", type(t.engine).__name__, "power =", t.engine.power)
print(rep.summary())
```
**Actual output:**
```
optimizers: [PartialClass(Opt, {'lr': 0.1})]
engines   : [Target(Engine, {})]
engine    : Engine power = 9
1 applied, 0 failed, 1 unused
```
**Expected:** `t.engines == [<Engine power=9>]` and `t.optimizers[0].kwargs == {'lr': 0.7}` — `confluid/fluid.py` `Target` docstring: "Materialization BUILDS it — always, and regardless of what surrounds it."; `AGENTS.md` → "The rule runs ONCE": "The active context's bare keys are read at construction for exactly what pass 7 cannot see — a marker born inside the constructor (a body slot `self.optimizer = PartialClass(...)`, a ctor default) — via `_broadcast_onto_instance`". A marker born inside the constructor inside a list is still a marker born inside the constructor; `AGENTS.md` → The I/O contract ("`List[Partial[T]]` marks the ELEMENT") names exactly this slot shape. The bare `lr` is at least reported `unused`; the unbuilt `Target` list is silent.
**Known?** NEW (BUGS-2026-08-13 C5 is the ctor-PARAM list on the configure path; this is the body slot on the load path)

## ENG-3 · S2 — A `_partial_: true` marker whose kwarg is `_ref_:` to a document node builds a SECOND referent when flowed later: `flow(sched).model is r["model"]` is False
**Where:** `confluid/engine.py:248-249` — `if isinstance(data, Target): return data if data.partial else flow(data)` — `instantiate` hands the `PartialClass` back with its settled kwargs still holding the referent MARKER; the instance memo that maps that marker to the instance built at top level dies with the pass (`:192-193` `_ENGINE_STATE.reset(token)`), so the later `flow()` rebuilds it at `:976` (`built = flow(v_copy)`) with `instance_memo is None`.
**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import configurable, load, flow
@configurable
class Model:
    def __init__(self, width: int = 1): self.width = width
@configurable
class Sched:
    def __init__(self, model=None, step: int = 1): self.model, self.step = model, step
r = load("model: {_target_: Model, width: 3}\nsched: {_target_: Sched, _partial_: true, model: {_ref_: model}}\n")
print("after load: sched =", type(r["sched"]).__name__, "| sched.kwargs['model'] =", r["sched"].kwargs["model"])
s = flow(r["sched"], step=5)
print("flow(sched).model is r['model'] ->", s.model is r["model"])
```
**Actual output:**
```
after load: sched = PartialClass | sched.kwargs['model'] = Target(Model, {'width': 3})
flow(sched).model is r['model'] -> False
```
**Expected:** one `Model` — `docs/targets.md`: "`!ref:` points at another node in the same document and yields the **same** live object wherever it appears" and `docs/lifecycle.md`: "After pass 7 nothing is late-bound … a reference to a marker is that marker" — the document built that marker; the deferred node should receive the instance it built, not a recipe for a second one.
**Known?** NEW (adjacent to BUGS-2026-08-19 ENG-12, which has the same symptom for a `Partial[T]` ctor slot but a different root — the memo-before-slot-check ordering)

## ENG-4 · S2 — A marker born in a constructor with a `Reference(...)` kwarg resolves against the RAW (pass-6) document, not the settled one: the child is a second, UN-broadcast instance (`width` 1 where the document's node has 7)
**Where:** `confluid/engine.py:1579-1580` — `if context and obj.target in context: return flow(context[obj.target], *runtime_args, **runtime_kwargs)` — `context` is the pass's `get_active_context()`, the PREPARED document whose markers carry their pre-pass-7 kwargs; reached from `_resolve_kwarg_value` `:986-988` (`if isinstance(v, Reference) and context: … return flow(v)`). The settled tree and its memo (`flow_memo` / `instance_memo`) are never consulted for the raw marker's id.
**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import configurable, load, Target, Reference
@configurable
class Model:
    def __init__(self, width: int = 1): self.width = width
@configurable
class Sched:
    def __init__(self, model=None): self.model = model
@configurable
class Trainer:
    def __init__(self): self.sched = Target(Sched, model=Reference("model"))     # a marker born in the constructor
r = load("model: {_target_: Model}\nwidth: 7\nt: {_target_: Trainer}\n")
print("document model.width =", r["model"].width)
print("t.sched.model is r['model'] ->", r["t"].sched.model is r["model"], "| t.sched.model.width =", r["t"].sched.model.width)
```
**Actual output:**
```
document model.width = 7
t.sched.model is r['model'] -> False | t.sched.model.width = 1
```
**Expected:** `t.sched.model is r["model"]` (width 7) — `docs/targets.md`: "yields the **same** live object wherever it appears"; `docs/lifecycle.md` pass 7: "every `${ref:}` is settled — a marker is shared by identity"; and the bare `width: 7` is a document key that "cascades tree-wide" (`AGENTS.md` → Flat-view ordered matching) — the referent handed to the body-slot marker never received it.
**Known?** NEW

## ENG-5 · S2 — Outside an active context a `Reference` kwarg is handed to the constructor RAW (silently), and a bare `flow(Reference("posixpath.join"))` raises — while the same import-path reference resolves under ANY `active_context`, even an unrelated one
**Where:** `confluid/engine.py:986` — `if isinstance(v, Reference) and context:` — with no context the value falls through to `:996-997` `if isinstance(v, Fluid): return v` and the `Reference` object becomes the constructor argument; `:1579` / `:1581` (`if context and …` / `if context:`) gate the exact-key AND the import-path resolution on a truthy context, so `resolve_reference_path` never runs without one.
**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import configurable, load, flow, Target, Reference, active_context
@configurable
class H:
    def __init__(self, fn=None): self.fn = fn
doc = "h: {_target_: H, fn: {_ref_: posixpath.join}}"       # the spelling dump() emits for a function-valued param
print("load(doc)['h'].fn                          ->", load(doc)["h"].fn)
print("flow(load(doc, until='document')['h']).fn  ->", flow(load(doc, until="document")["h"]).fn)
print("flow(Target(H, fn=Reference('posixpath.join'))).fn ->", flow(Target(H, fn=Reference("posixpath.join"))).fn)
try: print("flow(Reference('posixpath.join'))            ->", flow(Reference("posixpath.join")))
except Exception as e: print("flow(Reference('posixpath.join'))            ->", type(e).__name__, e)
with active_context({"unrelated": 1}):
    print("same, under ANY active_context              ->", flow(Reference("posixpath.join")))
```
**Actual output:**
```
load(doc)['h'].fn                          -> <function join at 0x1026ad620>
flow(load(doc, until='document')['h']).fn  -> Reference(posixpath.join, {})
flow(Target(H, fn=Reference('posixpath.join'))).fn -> Reference(posixpath.join, {})
flow(Reference('posixpath.join'))            -> ReferenceResolutionError Cannot resolve Reference: posixpath.join
same, under ANY active_context              -> <function join at 0x1026ad620>
```
**Expected:** `H.fn is posixpath.join` on every row — `AGENTS.md` → ONE path grammar: "anything else is an IMPORT PATH (`!ref:posixpath.join` — the spelling `dump()` emits for a function-valued param, and the reason import refs stay)"; an import path needs no document to resolve, and a constructor must never receive a `Reference` object where a function belongs (`docs/lifecycle.md`: "After pass 7 nothing is late-bound"). Resolution must not depend on whether an unrelated context happens to be active.
**Known?** NEW

## ENG-6 · S2 — A ctor-DEFAULT marker on a `__slots__` class is still shared across every host built in one pass (two Cars, one Engine) — the ENG-16 per-host copy lives only in the `__dict__` loop
**Where:** `confluid/engine.py:1503-1505` — `for param_name in params - seen: if param_name not in ctor: attr_val = getattr(instance, param_name, None)` — the second sweep (the one a `__slots__` instance takes, `instance_vars` being `None`) has no counterpart of the `:1472` guard `if class_defaults.get(attr_name) is attr_val:` that copies the shared default, so `_resolve_kwarg_value` memoizes on the class-level marker's id and the second host gets a memo HIT.
**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import configurable, load, Target, dump
@configurable
class Engine:
    def __init__(self, power: int = 1): self.power = power
@configurable
class Car:
    __slots__ = ("engine",)
    def __init__(self, engine=Target(Engine)): self.engine = engine
@configurable
class PlainCar:
    def __init__(self, engine=Target(Engine)): self.engine = engine
r = load("a: {_target_: Car}\nb: {_target_: Car}\nc: {_target_: PlainCar}\nd: {_target_: PlainCar}\n")
print("__slots__ Car : a.engine is b.engine ->", r["a"].engine is r["b"].engine)
print("plain     Car : c.engine is d.engine ->", r["c"].engine is r["d"].engine)
print(dump({"a": r["a"], "b": r["b"]}).strip().replace("\n", " | "))
```
**Actual output:**
```
__slots__ Car : a.engine is b.engine -> True
plain     Car : c.engine is d.engine -> False
a: |   _target_: Car |   engine: &id001 |     _target_: Engine |     power: 1 | b: |   _target_: Car |   engine: *id001
```
**Expected:** `False` for both — `AGENTS.md` → "a ctor-DEFAULT marker builds ONE child per host (2026-08-19, ENG-16)": "A default is evaluated once at class definition, so the id()-keyed instance memo handed every host in one pass the FIRST host's built child (two Cars, one Engine) — where the body-slot spelling and the same code outside a pass both build per host." The dump then faithfully emits the wrong graph as an anchor (`&id001` / `*id001`), so the sharing survives a round trip.
**Known?** NEW (the `__slots__` face of FIXED ENG-16 — the fix covered one of the two sweeps)

## ENG-7 · S2 — A ctor-DEFAULT marker stored under a DIFFERENT attribute name (`self.motor = engine`) is shared across hosts — the per-host copy is keyed on the attribute NAME, not the default OBJECT
**Where:** `confluid/engine.py:1472` — `if class_defaults.get(attr_name) is attr_val:` — `class_defaults` is keyed by the slot (parameter) name `engine`; the instance attribute is `motor`, so the lookup misses, no copy is made, and the memo at `:978` (`instance_memo[id(v)] = built`) keys the class-level default.
**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import configurable, load, Target
@configurable
class Engine:
    def __init__(self, power: int = 1): self.power = power
@configurable
class Car:
    def __init__(self, engine=Target(Engine)): self.motor = engine      # stored under a different attribute name
@configurable
class Car2:
    def __init__(self, engine=Target(Engine)): self.engine = engine     # stored under the param name (the ENG-16 fix shape)
r = load("a: {_target_: Car}\nb: {_target_: Car}\nc: {_target_: Car2}\nd: {_target_: Car2}\n")
print("renamed  : a.motor  is b.motor  ->", r["a"].motor is r["b"].motor)
print("same name: c.engine is d.engine ->", r["c"].engine is r["d"].engine)
```
**Actual output:**
```
renamed  : a.motor  is b.motor  -> True
same name: c.engine is d.engine -> False
```
**Expected:** `False` for both — `docs/targets.md`: "A marker used as a **constructor default** … is a *recipe*, not a shared node: Python evaluates the default once, but each host built in a pass gets its **own** child from it". The identity test should be against the SET of class-level default objects, not a name lookup.
**Known?** NEW (second face of FIXED ENG-16)

## ENG-8 · S2 — `load(x, solidify=False)` followed by `flow(root)` fires `solidify()` on the ROOT ONLY: the parent finalizes over un-finalized children (post-order violated on the live-object path)
**Where:** `confluid/engine.py:668-670` — `if not isinstance(obj, (Fluid, str, type, dict)): _maybe_solidify(obj); return obj` — the live-object return calls the hook on the one object handed in and never descends; the marker path gets post-order only because children are built (and solidified) inside `_resolve_kwarg_value` before `_construct`.
**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import configurable, load, flow, configure
@configurable
class Model:
    def __init__(self, width: int = 2): self.width, self.params = width, None
    def solidify(self):
        if self.params is None: self.params = list(range(self.width))
@configurable
class Trainer:
    def __init__(self, model=None): self.model, self.n_params = model, None
    def solidify(self): self.n_params = len(self.model.params) if self.model.params is not None else "EMPTY"
doc = "t: {_target_: Trainer, model: {_target_: Model}}"
t = load(doc, solidify=False)["t"]; flow(t)
print("load(solidify=False) then flow(t): n_params =", t.n_params, "| model.params =", t.model.params)
print("plain load                       : n_params =", load(doc)["t"].n_params)
t2 = load(doc, solidify=False)["t"]; configure(t2, config={})
print("load(solidify=False) then configure(t2, config={}): n_params =", t2.n_params)
```
**Actual output:**
```
load(solidify=False) then flow(t): n_params = EMPTY | model.params = None
plain load                       : n_params = 2
load(solidify=False) then configure(t2, config={}): n_params = 2
```
**Expected:** `n_params = 2` after `flow(t)` — `docs/lifecycle.md` pass 9: "`solidify()` fires post-order — children final, then the parent"; `docs/architecture.md` record 2: "The idempotency return calls `_maybe_solidify(obj)` … so the hook fires whichever way the object arrived" — and its motivating failure is exactly this one ("an optimizer flowed with `params=model.parameters()` got an empty parameter list, because the backbone `solidify()` would have built did not exist yet"). The CON row shows `configure(t2, config={})` IS post-order on the same live tree, so the two finalize paths disagree.
**Known?** NEW

## ENG-9 · S3 — `load()` EXECUTES a read-only property getter: a constructor param stored under a private name and exposed by `@property` (the documented class-design shape) runs its getter inside the pass — an expensive materialization, or a crash
**Where:** `confluid/engine.py:1505` — `attr_val = getattr(instance, param_name, None)` — the second sweep reads every ctor param the ctor call did not receive by `getattr`, which runs a class-level `property` (and any `__getattr__` proxy); the first sweep and the post-init path read `__dict__` only.
**Repro:**
```python
import traceback
from confluid import configurable, load
calls = []
@configurable
class Source:
    def __init__(self, data=None, split: str = "train"):
        self._data = data                      # the param is stored under a private name ...
        self.split = split
    @property
    def data(self):                            # ... and exposed by a read-only property (docs/class-design.md rule 3)
        calls.append("getter ran")
        raise RuntimeError("expensive materialization ran inside load()")
try:
    load("s: {_target_: Source, split: val}")
except Exception:
    print("".join(traceback.format_exc().splitlines(keepends=True)[-6:]).rstrip())
print("getter calls:", calls)
```
**Actual output:**
```
  File "/Users/gert/taidal/confluid/confluid/engine.py", line 1505, in _broadcast_onto_instance
    attr_val = getattr(instance, param_name, None)
               ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^
  File "/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-gert-taidal/7b14aa64-a2bc-4de0-847a-9e897af1e1a4/scratchpad/hunt/ENG/work/final/r02_property_getter.py", line 12, in data
    raise RuntimeError("expensive materialization ran inside load()")
RuntimeError: expensive materialization ran inside load()
getter calls: ['getter ran']
```
**Expected:** the object loads and the getter never runs — `docs/class-design.md`: "The configuration machinery itself never executes property getters — both `flow()` and `configure()` walk `vars(obj)` (instance attributes only)."; `AGENTS.md` → ONE read rule: "a slot shadowed by a class-level `property` is derived state — the getter NEVER runs (dump, discovery walk, and apply alike)". The crash is also unlocated (no `file:line:col`).
**Known?** NEW (BUGS-2026-08-13 C6 was the configure()-path getter; this is the load path)

## ENG-10 · S3 — A validating `__setattr__` that raises anything but `TypeError`/`AttributeError` escapes RAW and unlocated — from the engine's own `__confluid_extra__` stamp even when the document carries no extra key, and from the post-init setattr when it does
**Where:** `confluid/engine.py:1351-1354` — `try: instance.__confluid_extra__ = extra_keys / except (TypeError, AttributeError): pass` — a `ValueError` from the class's `__setattr__` is not in the clause, so the engine's bookkeeping write crashes a legal load; `:1333-1334` — `setattr(instance, k, v) / except (AttributeError, TypeError) as exc:` — same gap on the post-init path (ENG-10 of 2026-08-19 added `TypeError` only).
**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import configurable, load
@configurable(capture=False, validate=False)
class Strict:                                   # a validating __setattr__ that raises ValueError (not TypeError/AttributeError)
    def __init__(self, lr: float = 0.1): object.__setattr__(self, "lr", lr)
    def __setattr__(self, k, v):
        if k != "lr": raise ValueError(f"Strict has no field {k!r}")
        object.__setattr__(self, k, v)
for doc in ("s: {_target_: Strict, lr: 0.2}", "s: {_target_: Strict, lr: 0.2, bogus: 1}"):
    try: print(repr(doc), "->", load(doc)["s"].lr)
    except Exception as e: print(repr(doc), "->", type(e).__name__, "|", e)
```
**Actual output:**
```
2026-08-22 05:19:02 | WARNING  | confluid.engine:_warn_undeclared:1425 | Strict has no attribute 'bogus' at <unicode string>:1:4 — set as a post-init attribute anyway. Declare it as a constructor parameter or an __init__-body slot, or remove it.
's: {_target_: Strict, lr: 0.2}' -> ValueError | Strict has no field '__confluid_extra__'
's: {_target_: Strict, lr: 0.2, bogus: 1}' -> ValueError | Strict has no field 'bogus'
```
**Expected:** the first document loads (`0.2`) — the `__confluid_extra__` stamp is engine bookkeeping and must degrade like the `__confluid_kwargs__` stamp does ("`__slots__` skips the stamp gracefully", `AGENTS.md` → Eager classes); the second raises a LOCATED error — `AGENTS.md`: "EVERY error raised while processing a document NAMES ITS `file:line:col`" and the ENG-10 rule: "a validating `__setattr__`'s `TypeError` joins the `AttributeError` wrap at BOTH post-init setattr sites" — the wrap needs to be exception-agnostic, not a two-name list. (Under the default `capture=True` the same class fails one step earlier inside the `@configurable` capture stamp — `Failed to construct Strict at <unicode string>:1:4: Strict has no field '__confluid_kwargs__'` — located, but the class is unloadable; that site is `decorators.py`, outside this area.)
**Known?** NEW (the ValueError face of FIXED ENG-10)

```
0 applied, 1 failed, 0 unused
```

**Where:** `confluid/engine.py:1089` — `return slot_names(target, frozenset({"keyword", "var_keyword"}))` — `positional_only` is excluded from the ctor filter, so `k: 5` is removed from the call at `:776`; it would reach the post-init setattr at `:1237` only if the constructor succeeded without it. @configurable
```python
class Win:
    def __init__(self, k, /, size: int = 1): self.k, self.size = k, size
try: print(load("w: {_target_: Win, k: 5, size: 2}")["w"].k)
except Exception as e: print(type(e).__name__, "|", " ".join(str(e).splitlines()[:3]))
```

```
ConstructionError | Failed to construct Win at <unicode string>:1:4: 1 validation error for WinConfig k   Field required [type=missing, input_value={'size': 2}, input_type=dict]
```

(`AGENTS.md` says "positional_only IS settable … falls through to a post-init `setattr`" — true only for a DEFAULTED one, measured: `def __init__(self, k=0, /)` + `k: 5` → `k == 5`.)

## ENG-17 · S4 — `cast()` has no positional runtime channel (and no `solidify=` parameter): `cast(node, Cls, a, b)` is a Python `TypeError`, while `flow(node, a, b)` works

```python
try: print(cast(PartialClass(Loaders, device="cpu"), Loaders, "a", "b").loaders)
except Exception as e: print("cast(node, Loaders, 'a', 'b') ->", type(e).__name__, "|", e)
```

```
flow(node, 'a', 'b').loaders -> ['a', 'b']
cast(node, Loaders, 'a', 'b') -> TypeError | cast() takes 2 positional arguments but 4 were given
```
**Expected:** `['a', 'b']` — `docs/introspection.md`: "`cast(node, Cls)` is `flow` with a type assertion: it materializes the node"; `docs/targets.md`: "`flow(node, *args)` … So `flow()` takes runtime *args as well as runtime *kwargs". ## ENG-18 · S4 — `flow()` of a list / dict / tuple of markers is a silent pass-through: `flow(self.optimizers, params=…)` on a `List[Partial[Opt]]` slot returns the unbuilt markers and drops the runtime kwargs
**Where:** `confluid/engine.py:668-670` — `if not isinstance(obj, (Fluid, str, type, dict)): _maybe_solidify(obj); return obj` — a list / tuple takes the live-object return (runtime args "DROPPED"); a dict falls through every `isinstance` to `:708` `return obj`. `instantiate` (`:252-255`) knows how to walk containers but is not public and takes no runtime args.
**Repro:**
```python
from typing import List
from confluid import configurable, flow, Target, PartialClass, Partial, load
class Opt:
    def __init__(self, params=None, lr: float = 0.1): self.params, self.lr = params, lr
@configurable
class Trainer:
    def __init__(self): self.optimizers: List[Partial[Opt]] = [PartialClass(Opt, lr=0.1), PartialClass(Opt, lr=0.2)]
t = load("t: {_target_: Trainer}")["t"]
print("flow(t.optimizers, params=[1]) ->", flow(t.optimizers, params=[1]))
print("flow({'o': PartialClass(Opt)})  ->", flow({"o": PartialClass(Opt)}))
print("flow((Target(Opt),))            ->", flow((Target(Opt),)))
```
**Actual output:**
```
flow(t.optimizers, params=[1]) -> [PartialClass(Opt, {'lr': 0.1}), PartialClass(Opt, {'lr': 0.2})]
flow({'o': PartialClass(Opt)})  -> {'o': PartialClass(Opt, {})}
flow((Target(Opt),))            -> (Target(Opt, {}),)
```
**Expected:** the markers inside are built with the runtime kwargs (or a located refusal naming the container) — `docs/targets.md`: "A deferred node is built later by calling **`flow(node, **runtime_kwargs)`** … with runtime kwargs winning over stored ones", and `AGENTS.md` → The I/O contract names `List[Partial[T]]` as a slot shape ("marks the ELEMENT"). Returning the recipe list unchanged, with `params` silently discarded, is the far-from-cause failure record 2 describes.
**Known?** NEW
