```
2026-08-22 05:14:36 | WARNING  | confluid.validation:validate_setattr:366 | Opt.lr: invalid value
1 validation error for OptConfig
lr
  Input should be a valid number, unable to parse string as a number [type=float_parsing, input_value='not-a-float', input_type=str]
    For further information visit https://errors.pydantic.dev/2.13/v/float_parsing
--- strict, configure_from_file ---
RAISED ValidationError | message: ['1 validation error for OptConfig', 'lr', "  Input should be a valid number, unable to parse string as a number [type=float_parsing, input_value='not-a-float', input_type=str]"]
--- strict, load() of the same value ---
RAISED ConstructionError | Failed to construct Opt at /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-gert-taidal/7b14aa64-a2bc-4de0-847a-9e897af1e1a4/scratchpad/hu
--- warn, configure_from_file ---
lr after: not-a-float
```

**Expected:** `AGENTS.md` → Typed exceptions: "EVERY error raised while processing a document NAMES ITS `file:line:col` … A config error without a location is not a usable error"; `docs/validation.md`: "for a document-driven construction the warning names the marker's `file:line:col`, the same location the strict path's `ConstructionError` carries." `configure_from_file` IS document-driven and knows its file; both its outcomes say `Opt.lr` and nothing else.

**Where:** `confluid/pydantic_export.py:487-489` — `registered = get_registry().key_for(target); dunder = getattr(target, "__name__", None); name = registered or (dunder …)` — the fallback is `__name__`, where `dumper._target_name` falls back to the importable dotted path.

# --- file: helper_mod.py ---
class Unreg:

# --- file: p08b_marker_default_load.py ---
import yaml
from confluid import configurable, to_pydantic, Target, dump, load
from confluid.partial import Partial
from helper_mod import Unreg

@configurable
class Host:
    def __init__(self, opt: Partial[Unreg] = Target(Unreg, lr=0.01)):
        self.opt = opt

default = to_pydantic(Host).model_json_schema()["properties"]["opt"]["default"]
print("schema default:", default)
print("dump() opt:", dump(Host()).splitlines()[1:4])
text = yaml.safe_dump({"h": {"_target_": "Host", "opt": default}})
print("yaml from schema default:\n" + text)
try:
    print("load ->", load(text))
except Exception as e:
    print("load RAISED", type(e).__name__, ":", str(e).splitlines()[0][:200])
```

**Actual output:**
```
schema default: {'_target_': 'Unreg', 'lr': 0.01}
dump() opt: ['opt:', '  _target_: helper_mod.Unreg', '  lr: 0.01']
yaml from schema default:
h:
  _target_: Host
  opt:
    _target_: Unreg
    lr: 0.01

load RAISED UnknownClassError : Cannot resolve class: Unreg at <unicode string>:4:5
```

**Expected:** the default is the plain-format form `dump()` emits — `AGENTS.md` → Schema export: "`_plain_marker_form` renders the reserved-key dict (registry name first, like `dump()`); a marker whose kwargs are not JSON-clean is excluded SILENTLY … — never published as a lie"; `AGENTS.md` → Serialization: "fall back to the dotted path for an unregistered target (still importable)". The published default does not reload.
**Known?** NEW

## N15 · S4 — `confluid_class_of()` names a `<locals>` / nested class by its raw `__qualname__` (`make.<locals>.Op`, `Outer.Inner`), a `_target_` nothing can load — the registry key and `dump()` say `Op`

**Where:** `confluid/pydantic_export.py:124-130` — `_qualname`: `name = getattr(cls, "__qualname__", None) or cls.__name__ … return f"{module}.{name}"` (bare `name` for `__main__`), written to the model at `:648`; the registry strips `<locals>` (`registry._claim_key`) and `dump()` asks `key_for()` first.

**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import configurable, confluid_class_of, to_pydantic, load, dump, get_registry

def make():
    @configurable
    class Op:
        def __init__(self, k: int = 1):
            self.k = k
    return Op
Op = make()
name = confluid_class_of(to_pydantic(Op))
print("confluid_class_of:", name, "| registry key_for:", get_registry().key_for(Op), "| dump() says:", dump(Op()).splitlines()[0])
try:
    print("load ->", load(f"o:\n  _target_: {name}\n  k: 2\n"))
except Exception as e:
    print("load RAISED", type(e).__name__, ":", str(e).splitlines()[0][:200])

class Outer:
    @configurable
    class Inner:
        def __init__(self, k: int = 1):
            self.k = k
print("nested confluid_class_of:", confluid_class_of(to_pydantic(Outer.Inner)), "| key_for:", get_registry().key_for(Outer.Inner))
```

**Actual output:**
```
confluid_class_of: make.<locals>.Op | registry key_for: Op | dump() says: _target_: Op
load RAISED UnknownClassError : Cannot resolve class: make.<locals>.Op at <unicode string>:2:3
nested confluid_class_of: Outer.Inner | key_for: Inner
```

**Expected:** `docs/schema-export.md`: "`confluid_class_of(Model)` names the origin class (the `_target_` target to emit)"; `AGENTS.md` → Serialization: "a target's dumpable name comes from the REGISTRY … Ask `registry.key_for()` first". The name the schema hands a serializer is one the loader refuses.
**Known?** NEW (R12 is the `resolve_class` side for nested paths; this is the exporter emitting a name the registry would not)

## N16 · S4 — A non-JSON constructor default (a class object, an `object()` sentinel, a dataclass `<factory>`) logs a WARNING through loggair on EVERY `model_json_schema()` and the default vanishes — the N15 defect for every non-marker default

**Where:** `confluid/pydantic_export.py:420` — `return converted_type, Field(default=default, **desc_kw)` hands the raw object to pydantic, whose `json_schema.py:2463` emits `PydanticJsonSchemaWarning` each read; `:411-419` special-cases only `isinstance(default, Fluid)`.

**Repro:**
```python
import warnings
from typing import Type
from confluid import configurable, to_pydantic
_MISSING = object()
class Base: ...
class Impl(Base): ...

@configurable
class S:
    def __init__(self, x=_MISSING, model_cls: Type[Base] = Impl):
        self.x = x; self.model_cls = model_cls

with warnings.catch_warnings(record=True) as w:
    warnings.simplefilter("always")
    sch = to_pydantic(S).model_json_schema()
    sch = to_pydantic(S).model_json_schema()
print("x schema:", sch["properties"]["x"], "| model_cls schema:", sch["properties"]["model_cls"])
print("warnings:", [str(x.message)[:100] for x in w])
```

**Actual output:**
```
x schema: {'title': 'X'} | model_cls schema: {'title': 'Model Cls'}
warnings: ['Default value <object object at 0x105074e20> is not JSON serializable; excluding default from JSON s', "Default value <class '__main__.Impl'> is not JSON serializable; excluding default from JSON schema [", 'Default value <object object at 0x105074e20> is not JSON serializable; excluding default from JSON s', "Default value <class '__main__.Impl'> is not JSON serializable; excluding default from JSON schema ["]
```
Outside the `catch_warnings` block the same warning reaches the log on every read, via loggair's warnings interception (measured in `p02_type_param.py`): `2026-08-22 05:11:52 | WARNING  | pydantic.json_schema:emit_warning:2463 | PydanticJsonSchemaWarning: Default value <class '__main__.Impl'> is not JSON serializable; excluding default from JSON schema [non-serializable-default] (…/pydantic/json_schema.py:2463)`.

**Expected:** `AGENTS.md` → "a marker DEFAULT publishes its plain-format form, silently (N15) … a marker whose kwargs are not JSON-clean is excluded SILENTLY via the factory default" — the same silent exclusion (or, for a class value, the dotted path `dump()` emits — `cls: builtins.str` in N7's output) for every other non-JSON default. A per-read WARNING on a normal-operation schema read is the log-level mandate's definition of noise.
**Known?** NEW (N15 is FIXED for `Fluid` defaults only)

## N17 · S4 — `parse_param_docs` is indentation-unaware: a continuation line containing `word:` (a value list, `Default:`, `Note:`) ends the entry, and section words (`Returns`, `Attributes`, `Default`) come back as parameter names

**Where:** `confluid/schema.py:425-428` — the entry regex's lookahead `(?=\n\s*\*{0,2}[\w_]+\s*(?:\([^\n]*\))?:|\n\s*\n|\Z)` terminates at ANY `name:` line regardless of indentation depth; `:430-433` store every match, including section headers.

**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import parse_param_docs

class B:
    """Thing.

    Args:
        lr: Learning rate.
            Default: 0.1.
        path: Output path.
    Returns:
        Nothing useful.
    """
print("B:", parse_param_docs(B))

class C:
    """Sink.

    Args:
        path: Where to write. Note: the directory must exist.
        mode: One of:
            append: keep going
            truncate: start over
    """
print("C:", parse_param_docs(C))

class A:
    """Trainer.

    Args:
        lr: Learning rate for SGD.
        epochs: Number of passes.

    Attributes:
        lr: The stored learning rate (may be rescaled).
        history: Loss history.
    """
print("A:", parse_param_docs(A))
```

**Actual output:**
```
B: {'lr': 'Learning rate.', 'Default': '0.1.', 'path': 'Output path.', 'Returns': 'Nothing useful.'}
C: {'path': 'Where to write. Note: the directory must exist.', 'mode': 'One of:', 'append': 'keep going', 'truncate': 'start over'}
A: {'lr': 'Learning rate for SGD.', 'epochs': 'Number of passes.', 'Attributes': 'lr: The stored learning rate (may be rescaled).', 'history': 'Loss history.'}
```

**Expected:** `mode`'s help is "One of: append: keep going truncate: start over" and `lr`'s is "Learning rate. Default: 0.1." — the deeper-indented lines belong to the entry above (Google style's own rule, and the rule `_parse_numpy_entries` at `schema.py:450-458` already applies for NumPy docstrings). `docs/schema-export.md`: "`parse_param_docs` … Returns: Mapping of parameter name to its parsed help text" — `Returns`, `Default`, `Attributes` are not parameter names, and a `--help` renderer iterating the mapping prints them as such.
**Known?** NEW (N12 fixed `**kwargs:` termination and nested-paren types; indentation was not addressed)

## N18 · S4 — A nested `def` / nested `class` inside `__init__` contributes PHANTOM body slots: `self.hits` of a local class and `self.count` of a callback become slots of the outer class (schema fields, accept-list) that no instance ever has

**Where:** `confluid/introspect.py:205` — `for node in ast.walk(tree):` descends into nested `FunctionDef` / `ClassDef` bodies, and `:195-203` (`_self_attr`) matches any `self.<name>` target by the NAME `self` alone.

**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import configurable, to_pydantic
from confluid.introspect import slots
from confluid.broadcast import accepts_key

@configurable
class Host:
    def __init__(self):
        class _State:
            def __init__(self):
                self.hits = 0          # a DIFFERENT self
        self.state = _State()
        def on_event(self):            # a callback with its own `self`
            self.count = 0
        self.callbacks = [on_event]
        self.fn = lambda: setattr(self, "late", 1)

print("slots:", [(s.name, s.kind) for s in slots(Host)])
print("to_pydantic fields:", list(to_pydantic(Host).model_fields))
print("accepts_key hits/count:", accepts_key(Host, "hits"), accepts_key(Host, "count"))
h = Host()
print("Host() has 'hits' attr:", hasattr(h, "hits"), "| has 'count':", hasattr(h, "count"))
```

**Actual output:**
```
slots: [('callbacks', 'body_slot'), ('count', 'body_slot'), ('fn', 'body_slot'), ('hits', 'body_slot'), ('late', 'body_slot'), ('state', 'body_slot')]
to_pydantic fields: ['callbacks', 'count', 'fn', 'hits', 'late', 'state']
accepts_key hits/count: True True
Host() has 'hits' attr: False | has 'count': False
```

**Expected:** `docs/class-design.md`: the schema "surfaces every non-underscore `self.<name> = …` slot declared by a `@configurable` class" — `hits` and `count` are declared by a local class and a local function, not by `Host`; a bare `hits:` key now lands silently on every `Host` (accept-list) and the form advertises two knobs the object never reads. (The docstring of `scan_init_body` pins "nested `if`/`for`/`try` bodies are included", not nested scopes.)
**Known?** NEW

try:

RAISED AttributeError : 'property' object has no attribute '__confluid_output__'

from confluid import configurable
@configurable
class R:

```

R(**{}) RAISED TypeError : R.__init__() missing 1 required positional argument: 'x'
R(**{'y': 2}) RAISED ValidationError : 1 validation error for RConfig

Surface that to pydantic so the user sees the structured `extra="forbid"` / required-field error"; `docs/validation.md`: "All three default to `"strict"` — pydantic `ValidationError` is raised."

## N21 · S4 — On the mixed (Fluid-beside-concrete) path the strict error names the MANGLED field (`seed_`) instead of the parameter (`_seed`); the all-concrete path names `_seed`

**Where:** `confluid/validation.py:296` — `model.__pydantic_validator__.validate_assignment(stub, field, value)` validates by FIELD name (`field_name_for` → `seed_`), so pydantic's error `loc` is the mangled name; the fast path at `:282` validates by alias and reports `_seed`.

**Repro:**
```python
from typing import Optional
from confluid import configurable, Target

@configurable
class Child:
    def __init__(self, n: int = 1): self.n = n

@configurable
class H:
    def __init__(self, _seed: int = 1, child: Optional[Child] = None):
        self._seed = _seed; self.child = child

for kw in ({"_seed": "bad", "child": Target(Child)}, {"_seed": "bad"}):
    try:
        H(**kw)
    except Exception as e:
        print(list(kw), "RAISED", type(e).__name__, ":", str(e).splitlines()[1:3])
```

**Actual output:**
```
['_seed', 'child'] RAISED ValidationError : ['seed_', "  Input should be a valid integer, unable to parse string as an integer [type=int_parsing, input_value='bad', input_type=str]"]
['_seed'] RAISED ValidationError : ['_seed', "  Input should be a valid integer, unable to parse string as an integer [type=int_parsing, input_value='bad', input_type=str]"]
```

**Expected:** `AGENTS.md` → Schema export: "a leading-underscore name … get[s] a mangled field name (`seed_`) with the real name as the field ALIAS — never dropped (the alias is what `model_validate(kwargs)` and the JSON schema speak)". The diagnostic a user reads names a field the class does not have.
**Known?** NEW

**Where:** `confluid/schema.py:494-496` — `entries = _parse_docstring(init.__doc__ …); if not entries: entries = _parse_docstring(klass.__doc__ …)` — the class block is consulted only when the init block is EMPTY; the two are never merged per key.

**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import configurable, parse_param_docs
@configurable
class A:

print("A:", parse_param_docs(A))
```

**Actual output:**
```
A: {'path': 'Where to write (init doc).'}
```

**Expected:** `{'lr': …, 'epochs': …, 'path': …}` — `docs/discovery.md`: "Document a constructor argument once, at the source — form-spec builders and node tooltips both read it"; the MRO merge at `schema.py:490-497` already unions per key across classes ("subclass entries winning per key"), but not across the two docstrings of ONE class, so two documented parameters lose their help.

## N23 · S4 — `@configurable` on a pydantic `BaseModel` subclass: the declared fields are invisible to every reader, and pydantic's `model_fields` / `model_config` / `model_computed_fields` are reported as settable class-attribute slots

**Where:** `confluid/introspect.py:102-104` — `init_callable` returns `BaseModel.__init__` (`(self, /, **data)` → `var_keyword`), and `:507-527` — the `dir()` scan admits `model_fields` etc. as `class_attr` (non-callable, non-underscore); pydantic publishes the real signature on the CLASS (`inspect.signature(Cfg)` → `(*, lr: float = 0.1, epochs: int = 3)`), which no reader consults.

**Repro:**
```python
from pydantic import BaseModel
from confluid import configurable, to_pydantic, get_hierarchy
from confluid.introspect import slots
from confluid.broadcast import declares_key, accepts_any_key

@configurable
class Cfg(BaseModel):
    lr: float = 0.1
    epochs: int = 3

print("slots:", [(s.name, s.kind) for s in slots(Cfg)][:12])
print("to_pydantic fields:", list(to_pydantic(Cfg).model_fields))
print("accepts_any_key:", accepts_any_key(Cfg), "| declares_key lr:", declares_key(Cfg, "lr"), "| declares_key model_fields:", declares_key(Cfg, "model_fields"))
print("get_hierarchy:", get_hierarchy(Cfg))
```

**Actual output:**
```
slots: [('data', 'var_keyword'), ('model_computed_fields', 'class_attr'), ('model_config', 'class_attr'), ('model_fields', 'class_attr')]
to_pydantic fields: []
accepts_any_key: True | declares_key lr: False | declares_key model_fields: True
get_hierarchy: {}
```

**Expected:** `lr` and `epochs` are the class's slots — `AGENTS.md`: "'Which slots does this target have?' has ONE answer"; instead `declares_key(Cfg, "lr")` is False while `declares_key(Cfg, "model_fields")` is True, and an addressed `model_config: {...}` key would be accepted as a settable attribute on a pydantic model.
**Known?** overlaps I3 (the `object.__init__`/`**kwargs` accept-everything face); the `model_*` class-attr misclassification is new

---

### Suspected, not reproduced — N

- I2 has changed SHAPE but is still open: `Node(child: Optional["Node"])` now constructs with `WARNING … Node: validation is OFF for this class — no schema mirror can be built (RecursionError: maximum recursion depth exceeded)` (the `except Exception` at `validation.py:322` catches it), while a direct `to_pydantic(Node)` still raises a raw `RecursionError` (`p26_i2_state.py`). Not refiled.
- reST (`:param x:`) and epydoc (`@param x:`) docstrings yield `{}` from `parse_param_docs` — `docs/schema-export.md` names Google and NumPy only, so this is out of contract, not a mis-parse.
- A settable `@property` (getter + setter) is a `class_attr` slot: `accepts_key` admits it, `configure()` sets it, but `to_pydantic` / `get_hierarchy` / `dump()` omit it. The dump half is ruled (CD1/CD2: "a slot shadowed by a class-level property is derived state — the getter NEVER runs"); the schema/hierarchy half follows `class_attr ∉ _FIELD_KINDS`. Listed, not filed.
- An Enum whose member VALUES are tuples (`OddEnum.A = (1, 2)`) is coerced to `Any` as documented, but its DEFAULT is published as the JSON list `[1, 2]`, which round-trips to `Cls(p=[1, 2])` — a plain list, not the member — without a validation error. Documented coercion; the default rendering is questionable, not measured against a real consumer.
- Two parameters that mangle to the SAME field name (`_seed` and `seed_`) would overwrite each other in `fields` (`pydantic_export.py:624`); not a plausible spelling, not run.
- `_is_opaque_type(origin)` + `_json_schemable` are consulted only in the `origin is None` branch (N8/N9 above); `Type[X]` and `Literal[EnumMember]` happen to render (`{'title': …}` / `const`) in pydantic 2.13 — not filed.
- `@configurable` on a `dict`/`list` subclass (`class Config(dict)`) reads the C slot-wrapper `__init__` → `(*args, **kwargs)` → accept-everything and `IntrospectionError` from `to_pydantic` (`get_type_hints` on a wrapper_descriptor). Same root as I3/N6; not separately run.
- `validate_kwargs` skips a kwarg whose container holds a Fluid ANYWHERE, so `items=[Target(Child), "notachild"]` validates nothing for the string — documented ("Any kwarg whose value contains a Fluid anywhere in its tree is treated as deferred and skipped").
- The thread stress (8 threads × 300 mixed `to_pydantic` / `Cls()` / `to_pydantic.cache_clear()` / `clear_pass_caches()` / `slots()` / `accepts_key()` with `sys.setswitchinterval(1e-5)`) reported `errors: 0`; `_slots_cache` (no lock) recomputes benignly on a race. No concurrency defect reproduced.
- Pydantic-less subprocess: every point degraded to off with the one-time log line, `dump()/load()/configure()/parse_param_docs/input_specs/get_hierarchy/sanitize_schema` all worked, `to_pydantic`/`confluid_class_of` raised the documented `ImportError`. (R13's `from confluid import *` not re-run.)
- `sanitize_schema` over a realistic mirror (`Union[A, B]`, `Optional[Tuple[int, int]]`, `Dict[str, A]`, `Literal`, `List[Optional[int]]`, a `None`-typed param, two same-named `Thing` classes from different packages) left no `$ref`, typed every object/array, and kept field descriptions (`p30_sanitize_validity.py`, `p14_same_name_defs.py`); the only sanitize defect seen is the already-open I6.
- The `cls`-name filter (N7) also skips a constructor parameter named `self`/`cls` on a class whose `__init__` uses a different receiver name (`def __init__(this, …)`): `this` becomes a keyword slot. Unconventional; not filed.

# R· registry / decorators / public surface / exceptions / bake / env
## R1 · S2 — Re-running a `@configurable` cell with a CHANGED tag leaves the dead class registered: the name turns ambiguous, every `!class:Name` in the notebook raises, and the dotted key serves the stale class
**Where:** `confluid/registry.py:418-428` — `_claim_key`: `existing = self._by_key.get(base); if existing is None or existing.cls is cls: return base` … `candidate = f"{base}~{suffix}"` — a second class with the SAME `module.QualName` is keyed `~2` and then `:458-465` `_store` appends it (`if entries: logger.debug(... now maps to N classes ...)`; `entries.append(entry)`) because its tags differ. Nothing asks whether `sys.modules[cls.__module__].<QualName>` still IS the old object, i.e. whether the old entry is a redefinition the module no longer reaches.
**Repro:**
```python
# Notebook re-run: redefine a @configurable class in __main__ with a CHANGED tag
from confluid import configurable, get_registry, load
R = get_registry()
src1 = '''
from confluid import configurable
@configurable
class Foo:
    def __init__(self, x: int = 1): self.x = x
'''
src2 = '''
from confluid import configurable
@configurable(role="loss")
class Foo:
    def __init__(self, x: int = 1): self.x = x
'''
g = {"__name__": "__main__"}
exec(src1, g); first = g["Foo"]
exec(src2, g); second = g["Foo"]
print("list_classes():", sorted(R.list_classes()))
print("get_class('__main__.Foo') is first(dead):", R.get_class("__main__.Foo") is first)
try:
    print("load:", load("o: !class:Foo\n  x: 2\n")["o"])
except Exception as e:
    print("load:", type(e).__name__ + ":", str(e).replace("\n", " | "))
```
**Actual output:**
```
list_classes(): ['__main__.Foo', '__main__.Foo~2']
get_class('__main__.Foo') is first(dead): True
load: AmbiguousClassError: 'Foo' is registered by 2 classes, so a bare lookup cannot choose between them: |   __main__.Foo |   __main__.Foo~2 (role=loss) | Disambiguate with the dotted path (_target_: __main__.Foo), a selector (_target_: Foo@role=None) or get_class('Foo', role=None). at <unicode string>:1:4
```
(The identical-tag re-run — same script with `src1` twice — replaces and logs `WARNING … 'Foo' is already registered by __main__.Foo with the same tags; __main__.Foo~2 REPLACES it. Give one a distinguishing tag (framework= / role= / group=) or an explicit name= to keep both.` — advice that, followed, produces exactly the state above. A third re-run with the original tags — measured in `p30_verify_claims.py`, `exec(src1); exec(src2); exec(src1)` — gives `list_classes(): ['__main__.Foo~2', '__main__.Foo~3']`: still ambiguous; only `clear()` recovers.)
**Expected:** a class whose `module.QualName` the module itself no longer binds to the old object is a REDEFINITION, not a namesake — the old entry is replaced regardless of tags, and `get_class('__main__.Foo')` answers the live class. AGENTS.md → "A registered NAME may map to SEVERAL classes": "Same class object → replace, silent. Same name + all five tags equal + different object → warning + last-write-wins. Any tag differing → coexist" — the coexist arm was written for "a class rebuilt by a decorator, or two defined in the same function scope" (`_claim_key` docstring), and the brief's lens "serves a stale class after redefinition" is exactly what `get_class('__main__.Foo') is first(dead): True` shows. The `@role=None` hint in the message is BUGS-2026-08-13 C12's known second face.
**Known?** NEW (C12 covers a rename leaving a stale OLD-NAME entry; this is a same-name redefinition leaving a stale SAME-NAME entry, and the dotted-key lookup serving it)
## R2 · S2 — A subclass cannot opt back in: `@configurable(broadcast=True)` / `strict_attrs=False` / `lazy=False` on a subclass of an opted-out parent are silently ignored
**Where:** `confluid/registry.py:326`, `:332`, `:334` — `lazy = lazy or bool(getattr(cls, "__confluid_partial__", False))`, `no_broadcast = no_broadcast or bool(getattr(cls, "__confluid_no_broadcast__", False))`, `strict_attrs = strict_attrs or bool(getattr(cls, "__confluid_strict_attrs__", False))` — `getattr` reads the PARENT's stamp through the MRO and `or` makes the explicit `False` argument indistinguishable from "not given"; `:390-397` then stamps the inherited value onto the subclass's own `__dict__`.
**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import configurable, marks, register
@configurable(broadcast=False, strict_attrs=True, lazy=True)
class P:
    def __init__(self, x: int = 1): self.x = x
@configurable(broadcast=True, strict_attrs=False, lazy=False)
class C(P):
    pass
m = marks(C)
print("C: no_broadcast=", m.no_broadcast, "strict_attrs=", m.strict_attrs, "lazy=", m.lazy)
print("C.__dict__ stamps:", sorted(k for k in C.__dict__ if k.startswith("__confluid")))
```
**Actual output:**
```
C: no_broadcast= True strict_attrs= True lazy= True
C.__dict__ stamps: ['__confluid_configurable__', '__confluid_name__', '__confluid_no_broadcast__', '__confluid_partial__', '__confluid_strict_attrs__']
```
**Expected:** `broadcast=True` on `C` means bare keys land on `C`; `strict_attrs=False` means `C` absorbs undeclared keys; `lazy=False` means composers build it. AGENTS.md → "Behavioral marks": "Each mark follows the fallback template (argument wins, else the existing mark survives)" — here the argument loses. Only `capture=False` is documented as "Inherited by subclasses" (`decorators.py:149`); the other opt-outs inherit silently AND are stamped onto the child, so a later `register(C, broadcast=True)` cannot clear them either (measured in `p30_verify_claims.py`: `after register(C, broadcast=True): marks(C).no_broadcast = True`).
**Known?** NEW
## R3 · S2 — `broadcast_attrs="optimizer"` (a bare string) is accepted and declares the slots `o, p, t, i, m, i, z, e, r`
**Where:** `confluid/registry.py:337-341` — `effective_broadcast_attrs = (tuple(broadcast_attrs) if broadcast_attrs is not None else …)` — `tuple("optimizer")` iterates the characters; the annotation `Optional[Sequence[str]]` (`decorators.py:36`, `:268`, `registry.py:265`) admits a `str`, so neither mypy nor the code refuses it.
**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import configurable, marks, accepts_key
@configurable(broadcast_attrs="optimizer")
class T:
    def __init__(self, lr: float = 0.1):
        self.lr = lr
print("marks(T).broadcast_attrs:", marks(T).broadcast_attrs)
print("accepts_key(T, 'o'):", accepts_key(T, "o"), "| accepts_key(T, 'optimizer'):", accepts_key(T, "optimizer"))
```
**Actual output:**
```
marks(T).broadcast_attrs: ('o', 'p', 't', 'i', 'm', 'i', 'z', 'e', 'r')
accepts_key(T, 'o'): True | accepts_key(T, 'optimizer'): False
```
**Expected:** a `str` is refused with `ConfigurableDefinitionError` (or wrapped as a one-element list); `optimizer` is the declared slot, `o` is not. AGENTS.md → "Behavioral marks": "`broadcast_attrs=[...]` | declares post-init body-slot names, UNIONED with the AST scan" — nine single-letter keys now cascade into `T` (`accepts_key(T, 'o') == True`) while the one name the author wrote is not declared, and in a frozen deployment (the mark's whole purpose) `optimizer` is invisible.
**Known?** NEW
## R4 · S3 — `!class:.Foo` / `resolve_class(".Foo")` crash with a raw `ValueError: Empty module name`; `..Foo` with a raw `TypeError` — untyped and unlocated
**Where:** `confluid/registry.py:793-802` — `if "." in base: module_path, class_attr = base.rsplit(".", 1); try: module = importlib.import_module(module_path) … except (ImportError, AttributeError) as e:` — `".Foo".rsplit(".", 1)` gives `module_path == ""`, `import_module("")` raises `ValueError`, `"..Foo"` gives `"."` which raises `TypeError`; neither is in the except tuple, so they escape `resolve_class` (documented "NON-raising by default") and `engine.py:844` `_resolve_target_callable` (which only re-locates `ConfigurationError`).
**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import load
from confluid.registry import resolve_class
for spec in (".Foo", "..Foo", "pkg..Foo"):
    try:
        print(f"resolve_class({spec!r}):", resolve_class(spec))
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"resolve_class({spec!r}):", type(e).__name__ + ":", e)
try:
    print("load:", load("o: !class:.Foo\n"))
except Exception as e:
    print("load('o: !class:.Foo'):", type(e).__name__ + ":", e)
```
**Actual output:**
```
resolve_class('.Foo'): ValueError: Empty module name
resolve_class('..Foo'): TypeError: the 'package' argument is required to perform a relative import for '.'
resolve_class('pkg..Foo'): None
load('o: !class:.Foo'): ValueError: Empty module name
```
**Expected:** `resolve_class` returns `None` (non-strict) and `load()` raises `UnknownClassError: Cannot resolve class: .Foo at <unicode string>:1:4`. AGENTS.md → "Typed exceptions": "Every error confluid raises MUST come from `confluid/exceptions.py` (root `ConfluidError`), never a bare builtin" and "EVERY error raised while processing a document NAMES ITS `file:line:col`". A leading dot is the typo a relative-import habit produces (`from .mod import Foo` → `!class:.mod.Foo`).
**Known?** NEW (BUGS-2026-08-19 R11 is the *module-raises-at-import* face; this is the import-path GRAMMAR face)
## R5 · S4 — A `@axis=value` selector that does not match is an error on a bare name but SILENTLY IGNORED on the dotted key of the same registered class; `get_class` and `resolve_class` disagree
**Where:** `confluid/registry.py:784-800` — `cls = _registry._select(base, wanted)` returns `None` for the filtered-out dotted key, and the code falls straight through to `if "." in base: … importlib.import_module(module_path); attr = getattr(module, class_attr); … return attr` — the import branch never looks at `wanted`. The docstring Note (`:761-763`) exempts "untagged imports"; this class is tagged AND registered.
**Repro:**
```python
import sys, pathlib
pathlib.Path("m7").mkdir(exist_ok=True)
pathlib.Path("m7/__init__.py").write_text("")
pathlib.Path("m7/losses.py").write_text(
    "from confluid import configurable\n"
    "@configurable(role='loss', framework='torch')\n"
    "class Hinge:\n"
    "    def __init__(self, x: int = 1): self.x = x\n")
sys.path.insert(0, ".")
import m7.losses
from confluid import load, get_registry
from confluid.registry import resolve_class
for spec in ("Hinge@role=metric", "m7.losses.Hinge@role=metric"):
    try:
        print(spec, "-> load:", load(f"o: !class:{spec}\n")["o"])
    except Exception as e:
        print(spec, "-> load:", type(e).__name__ + ":", str(e).split("\n")[0][:140])
print("get_class('m7.losses.Hinge', role='metric'):", get_registry().get_class("m7.losses.Hinge", role="metric"))
print("resolve_class('m7.losses.Hinge@role=metric'):", resolve_class("m7.losses.Hinge@role=metric"))
```
**Actual output:**
```
Hinge@role=metric -> load: UnknownClassError: Cannot resolve class: Hinge@role=metric at <unicode string>:1:4 — no class named 'Hinge' with role='metric' is registered
m7.losses.Hinge@role=metric -> load: <m7.losses.Hinge object at 0x1063fe660>
get_class('m7.losses.Hinge', role='metric'): None
resolve_class('m7.losses.Hinge@role=metric'): <class 'm7.losses.Hinge'>
```
**Expected:** the dotted spelling refuses like the bare one (`no class named 'm7.losses.Hinge' with role='metric' is registered`), or `get_class` answers the class like `resolve_class` does — one of the two, not both. docs/discovery.md → "When two classes share a name": "an unknown axis is rejected rather than silently ignored"; AGENTS.md → "Config-side disambiguation": "An unknown axis raises `ConfigurationError` rather than selecting nothing" — a non-matching VALUE on a registered class selects the wrong thing silently, which is the outcome the selector exists to prevent.
**Known?** NEW
## R6 · S4 — `@group=2` (literal) matches the tag `"2"`, but `@group=$ver` with `ver: 2` does not, and the refusal says `group=2 … is not registered` while `group=2` is
**Where:** `confluid/registry.py:171` — `out[axis] = found` stores the YAML-typed value (`int` 2) untouched; `:542` `getattr(entry, axis) == value` compares `"2" == 2` → `False`; `engine.py:875` `_selector_detail` renders `f"{axis}={value!r}"` so the int prints as `2`, indistinguishable from the working literal in the same message.
**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import configurable, load
@configurable(category="op", group="2")
class Op:
    def __init__(self, x: int = 1): self.x = x
@configurable(category="op", group="3")
class Op:  # noqa: F811
    def __init__(self, x: int = 1): self.x = x
print("literal  :", load("o: !class:Op@group=2\n")["o"].__class__)
try:
    print("$ver (int):", load("ver: 2\no: !class:Op@group=$ver\n")["o"])
except Exception as e:
    print("$ver (int):", type(e).__name__ + ":", str(e).split("\n")[0])
```
**Actual output:**
```
literal  : <class '__main__.Op'>
$ver (int): UnknownClassError: Cannot resolve class: Op@group=$ver at <unicode string>:2:4 — no class named 'Op' with group=2 is registered
```
**Expected:** the two spellings agree — the `$key` value is compared as the string the tag is (tags are always `str`, `_ClassEntry` fields are `Optional[str]`), or the message says `group=2 (int; registered tags are strings)`. docs/discovery.md: "A value written `$key` is read from the loaded configuration — so a document says which engine it is targeting once"; `engine.py:868-872` `_selector_detail`'s own rationale: "Explain a selector miss in terms of the RESOLVED filter … which hides the thing the reader needs: which value `$framework` actually carried" — the message still hides the one thing that differs (the type).
**Known?** NEW

**Where:** `confluid/registry.py:163-164` — `path = value[1:]` is `""`, and `Resolver(context=context)._lookup_path("", context)` answers the root mapping, which is `not None`, so `:171` stores the document as the axis value; `:166-170`'s "could not be resolved" guard never fires.

@configurable(framework="torch")
class CE:

class KCE:

```

'loss: !class:CE@framework=$m.x' -> ConfigurationError: selector framework=$m.x could not be resolved — no key 'm.x' in the active configuration. Define it (e.g. `m.x: torch` at the top level, or inside the matching scope block) or write the value literally. at <unicode string>:3:7
'loss: !class:CE@framework=$nope' -> ConfigurationError: selector framework=$nope could not be resolved — no key 'nope' in the active configuration. Define it (e.g. `nope: torch` at the top level, or inside the matching scope block) or write the value literally. at <unicode string>:1:7
'loss: !class:CE@framework=$' -> UnknownClassError: Cannot resolve class: CE@framework=$ at <unicode string>:1:7 — no class named 'CE' with framework={'loss': Target(CE@framework=$, {})} is registered
```
(First two lines show the located, correct refusals for comparison — the empty-path case is the only wrong one.)
**Expected:** `$` with no key is a malformed selector → `ConfigurationError: malformed class selector in 'CE@framework=$' …` like the other malformed pairs (`registry.py:130-133`), located. `parse_target_spec`'s contract (`registry.py:119-121`): "Raises: ConfigurationError: on a malformed pair … must fail loudly, not select nothing." On a real config the message would carry the whole document (every marker repr) in one line.

## R9 · S4 — A `register()`-ed callable INSTANCE or `functools.partial` gets a registry key containing `<`, spaces and `(`; `list_classes()` advertises it once the name is shared and `!class:<key>` dies with a raw PyYAML `ScannerError`; a `@configurable` partial is keyed `functools._wrap_callable_with_validation.wrapper`
**Where:** `confluid/registry.py:98-101` — `qualname = getattr(cls, "__qualname__", None) or getattr(cls, "__name__", None) or repr(cls)`; `tag_legal = re.sub(r"<(\w+)>", r"\1", …)` — the regex only unwraps a bare `<word>`, so `repr()` of an instance (`<__main__.Fn object at 0x…>`) or of a partial (`functools.partial(<function base at 0x…>, x=2)`) survives with `<`, spaces and parentheses. For the `@configurable` path, `decorators.py:383` `@functools.wraps(func)` copies `__module__` (`functools`) off the partial but no `__qualname__`, so every named partial keys as `functools._wrap_callable_with_validation.wrapper` (the next one `~2`).
**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import register, get_registry, load
R = get_registry()
class Fn:
    def __call__(self, x: int = 1): return x
register(Fn(), name="Inst", role="a"); register(Fn(), name="Inst", role="b")
keys = sorted(k for k in R.list_classes(role="a"))
print("list_classes(role='a'):", keys)
k = keys[0]
print("get_class(key):", R.get_class(k))
try:
    print("load('o: !class:<key>'):", load(f"o: !class:{k}\n"))
except Exception as e:
    print("load('o: !class:<key>'):", type(e).__name__ + ":", str(e).split("\n")[0])
try:
    print("load(reserved-key):", load(f"o: {{_target_: '{k}'}}\n"))
except Exception as e:
    print("load(reserved-key):", type(e).__name__ + ":", str(e).split("\n")[0])
```
**Actual output:**
```
list_classes(role='a'): ['__main__.<__main__.Fn object at 0x107feaf30>']
get_class(key): <__main__.Fn object at 0x107feaf30>
load('o: !class:<key>'): ScannerError: while scanning a tag
load(reserved-key): {'o': 1}
```
Second face (same probe family, `p10_callable_instance_key.py`):
```python
import functools
from confluid import register, get_registry, configurable
R = get_registry()
def base(x: int = 1): return x
register(functools.partial(base, x=2), name="Part")
print("key(Part):", [e.key for es in R._entries.values() for e in es if e.name == "Part"])
w = configurable(name="WPart")(functools.partial(base, x=3))
print("key(WPart):", [e.key for es in R._entries.values() for e in es if e.name == "WPart"], "| module:", w.__module__, "| name:", w.__name__)
```
```
key(Part): ['functools.functools.partial(<function base at 0x104b38cc0>, x=2)']
key(WPart): ['functools._wrap_callable_with_validation.wrapper'] | module: functools | name: wrapper
```
**Expected:** a tag-legal key (e.g. `__main__.Fn` for an instance of `Fn`, `__main__.base` for a partial of `base`, `~N`-suffixed when shared) — `registry.py:71-72` `_entry_key` docstring: "The canonical dotted key for `cls` — ALWAYS contains a `.`, never a `<`"; AGENTS.md → Registry: "`_by_key` (canonical `module.QualName`, `<locals>` stripped, tag-legal)". A `list_classes()` key that `!class:` cannot parse is the exact defect the no-`<` invariant was written against (`registry.py:93-96`), and `ScannerError` is a raw PyYAML exception.
**Known?** NEW (BUGS-2026-08-19 R1/R2 fixed the CRASH and the `wrapper` NAME for nameless callables; the KEY is the residue)
## R10 · S4 — A registration `name=` equal to another class's dotted key shadows it: `list_classes()` publishes `m14.a.Foo` for the class in `m14.a`, `get_class('m14.a.Foo')` returns a different class
**Where:** `confluid/registry.py:531-535` — `_candidates`: `entries = self._entries.get(name); if entries: return list(entries)` is consulted BEFORE `self._by_key.get(name)`, and `:297-306` never refuses a `name` that collides with an existing dotted key.
**Repro:**
```python
import sys, pathlib
pathlib.Path("m14").mkdir(exist_ok=True); pathlib.Path("m14/__init__.py").write_text("")
pathlib.Path("m14/a.py").write_text("from confluid import configurable\n@configurable(group='g1')\nclass Foo:\n    def __init__(self, x: int = 1): self.x = x\n")
pathlib.Path("m14/b.py").write_text("from confluid import configurable\n@configurable(group='g2')\nclass Foo:\n    def __init__(self, x: int = 1): self.x = x\n")

from confluid import configurable, get_registry
R = get_registry()
@configurable(name="m14.a.Foo")
class Bar:
    def __init__(self, x: int = 1): self.x = x
print("list_classes():", sorted(k for k in R.list_classes() if "Foo" in k))
print("get_class('m14.a.Foo'):", R.get_class("m14.a.Foo"))
```
**Actual output:**
```
list_classes(): ['m14.a.Foo', 'm14.b.Foo']
get_class('m14.a.Foo'): <class '__main__.Bar'>
```

That contract is what the whole enumerate-then-look-up idiom rests on"; `registry.py:7-8`: "gives each entry a canonical dotted key (`module.QualName`) that is unique by construction" — a dotted `name=` breaks the uniqueness the key is built on.

## R12 · S4 — Without pydantic, `hasattr(confluid, "to_pydantic")` and `getattr(confluid, "to_pydantic", None)` RAISE `ImportError` instead of answering `False` / the default
**Where:** `confluid/__init__.py:153-163` — `def __getattr__(name)` raises `ImportError` for the two lazy names; `hasattr`/`getattr(..., default)` only swallow `AttributeError`, so the standard feature-probe idiom raises.

sys.modules["pydantic"] = None

for probe in ("hasattr(confluid, 'to_pydantic')", "getattr(confluid, 'to_pydantic', None)"):

hasattr(confluid, 'to_pydantic') -> ImportError: confluid.to_pydantic requires pydantic, which is an optional dependency — install the extra: pip install 'confluid[pydantic]'
getattr(confluid, 'to_pydantic', None) -> ImportError: confluid.to_pydantic requires pydantic, which is an optional dependency — install the extra: pip install 'confluid[pydantic]'
```

**Known?** overlaps BUGS-2026-08-19 R13 (same `__getattr__`; R13 filed the star-import and `dir()` faces, not the `hasattr`/default-`getattr` face)
## R13 · S4 — `load_workspace_env` says "DATA_ROOT is not set -- add it to <.env>" when the `.env` file HAS it (the shell exported an empty value and `override=False` keeps it), and says the same when `.env` is a directory
**Where:** `confluid/env.py:57-61` — `if probe.exists(): env_path = probe; load_dotenv(env_path, override=override)` — `exists()` is true for a directory and `load_dotenv` then silently loads nothing; `:71-73` — `value = os.environ.get(key); if not value: raise WorkspaceEnvError(f"{key} is not set -- add it to {env_path} …")` — the message names the file as the place to fix, but with `override=False` python-dotenv does not replace an existing (empty) `os.environ` value, so the `.env` line is correct and ignored.
**Repro:**
```python
import os, pathlib
from confluid.env import load_workspace_env
root = pathlib.Path("env17b").resolve(); (root / "sub").mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
(root / ".env").write_text("DATA_ROOT=/tmp\n")
os.environ["DATA_ROOT"] = ""          # e.g. `export DATA_ROOT=` left in a shell profile
try:
    print("shell exported '' + .env has /tmp ->", load_workspace_env(root / "sub"))
except Exception as e:
    print("shell exported '' + .env has /tmp ->", type(e).__name__ + ":", e)
os.environ.pop("DATA_ROOT", None)
(root / ".env").unlink(); (root / ".env").mkdir()
try:
    print(".env is a directory ->", load_workspace_env(root / "sub"))
except Exception as e:
    print(".env is a directory ->", type(e).__name__ + ":", e)
```
**Actual output:**
```
shell exported '' + .env has /tmp -> WorkspaceEnvError: DATA_ROOT is not set -- add it to /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-gert-taidal/7b14aa64-a2bc-4de0-847a-9e897af1e1a4/scratchpad/hunt/R/work/env17b/.env (e.g. DATA_ROOT=/Volumes/Store).
.env is a directory -> WorkspaceEnvError: DATA_ROOT is not set -- add it to /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-gert-taidal/7b14aa64-a2bc-4de0-847a-9e897af1e1a4/scratchpad/hunt/R/work/env17b/.env (e.g. DATA_ROOT=/Volumes/Store).
```

BUGS-2026-08-19 PA7 already ruled "an EMPTY env var is treated as unset" for the loader — this helper treats it as SET (it blocks the `.env` value) and then reports it as unset.

## R14 · S4 — Two distributions declaring the same entry-point NAME: both modules import, but `load_configurables()` silently returns only the last one
**Where:** `confluid/registry.py:710-712` — `for ep in importlib.metadata.entry_points(group=group): try: loaded[ep.name] = ep.load()` — `ep.name` is per-distribution, not unique across the group; the second assignment overwrites the first with no diagnostic.
**Repro:**
```python
import sys, pathlib
site = pathlib.Path("site18").resolve(); site.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
for pkg in ("pa", "pb"):
    (site / pkg).mkdir(exist_ok=True)
    (site / pkg / "__init__.py").write_text(f"from confluid import configurable\n@configurable(name='{pkg.upper()}')\nclass X:\n    def __init__(self): pass\n")
    d = site / f"{pkg}-1.0.dist-info"; d.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
    (d / "METADATA").write_text(f"Metadata-Version: 2.1\nName: {pkg}\nVersion: 1.0\n")
    (d / "entry_points.txt").write_text(f"[confluid.configurables]\nops = {pkg}\n")
sys.path.insert(0, str(site))
import importlib.metadata as m
print("entry points seen:", [(e.name, e.value) for e in m.entry_points(group="confluid.configurables") if e.value in ("pa", "pb")])
from confluid import load_configurables, get_registry
loaded = load_configurables()
print("returned for 'ops':", loaded.get("ops"))
print("registered:", sorted(k for k in get_registry().list_classes() if k in ("PA", "PB")))
```
**Actual output:**
```
entry points seen: [('ops', 'pa'), ('ops', 'pb')]
returned for 'ops': <module 'pb' from '/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-gert-taidal/7b14aa64-a2bc-4de0-847a-9e897af1e1a4/scratchpad/hunt/R/work/site18/pb/__init__.py'>
registered: ['PA', 'PB']
```
**Expected:** every imported entry is reported (key by `f"{ep.dist.name}:{ep.name}"` or `ep.value`, or warn on the collision). `registry.py:704-705`: "Returns: `{entry_name: module}` for each entry that imported, with the raised exception instance in place of the module for each entry that failed" — a FAILED `pa` whose name `pb` also uses would vanish from the dict entirely, defeating the per-entry error reporting the docstring promises (docs/discovery.md: "reported in the returned dict as the exception instance").
**Known?** NEW
## R15 · S4 — `display_name` is inherited from the parent AND stamped onto the subclass's own `__dict__`: `marks(Child).display_name == "Fancy Parent"`
**Where:** `confluid/registry.py:331` — `display_name = display_name if display_name is not None else getattr(cls, "__confluid_display_name__", None)` reads the PARENT's label through the MRO; `:388-389` `if display_name is not None: setattr(cls, "__confluid_display_name__", display_name)` then stamps it on the child, so even a consumer reading `cls.__dict__` gets the parent's label. Contrast `:297` for `name`, which deliberately reads `cls.__dict__` only.
**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import configurable, get_registry, marks
@configurable(name="FancyP", display_name="Fancy Parent")
class Parent:
    def __init__(self, x: int = 1): self.x = x
@configurable
class Child(Parent):
    pass
m = marks(Child)
print("Child registered name:", get_registry().key_for(Child))
print("marks(Child).name:", m.name, "| marks(Child).display_name:", m.display_name)
print("Child.__dict__ has display_name stamp:", "__confluid_display_name__" in Child.__dict__)
```
**Actual output:**
```
Child registered name: Child
marks(Child).name: Child | marks(Child).display_name: Fancy Parent
Child.__dict__ has display_name stamp: True
```
**Expected:** `marks(Child).display_name is None` (the GUI falls back to `Child`). `decorators.py:169-171`: "display_name: Optional human-readable label for UI surfaces … Falls back to the class name when absent." AGENTS.md → Registry: "`register_class`'s `name` falls back to the class's OWN `__dict__` mark, never an inherited one" — `display_name` is the same kind of per-class identity and takes the opposite rule, so a palette shows two entries labelled "Fancy Parent".
**Known?** NEW
## R16 · S4 — The `constant`/`random` contradiction is refused only by the decorator: a subclass of a `random=True` class may declare `constant=True`, and `register_class(cls, constant=True)` on a `random=True` class stamps both
**Where:** `confluid/decorators.py:179-183` — the only check (`if constant and random: raise ConfigurableDefinitionError`) sees the decorator's own arguments; `confluid/registry.py:327-328` — `random = random or bool(getattr(cls, "__confluid_random__", False)); constant = constant or bool(getattr(cls, "__confluid_constant__", False))` merge the inherited/existing mark in AFTER that check and `:375-378` stamp both without looking.
**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import configurable, get_registry, marks
@configurable(random=True)
class P:
    def __init__(self): pass
try:
    @configurable(constant=True)
    class C(P):
        pass
    print("subclass: constant=", marks(C).constant, "random=", marks(C).random)
except Exception as e:
    print("subclass:", type(e).__name__, e)
@configurable(random=True)
class Q:
    def __init__(self): pass
try:
    get_registry().register_class(Q, constant=True)
    print("register_class(Q, constant=True) after random=True: constant=", marks(Q).constant, "random=", marks(Q).random)
except Exception as e:
    print("register_class:", type(e).__name__, e)
```
**Actual output:**
```
subclass: constant= True random= True
register_class(Q, constant=True) after random=True: constant= True random= True
```
**Expected:** `ConfigurableDefinitionError` in both cases. AGENTS.md → "Behavioral marks": "`constant=True` | … Mutually exclusive with `random` (raises at decoration time)" and "`registry.register_class` is the ONE stamping authority for every `__confluid_*__` mark" — the authority that stamps the pair is the one place that does not check it; an exporter reading `marks(C)` now sees a node that is both foldable and must re-execute every run.
**Known?** NEW
## R17 · S4 — A partial re-register of an UNSTAMPABLE target (a builtin) drops its tags: `register(dict, name="Mapping", role="sink")` then `register_class(dict, name="Mapping")` empties `list_classes(role="sink")`
**Where:** `confluid/registry.py:311-325` — every fallback reads the CLASS stamp (`getattr(cls, "__confluid_role__", None)` …), never the existing `_ClassEntry`; `:406-408` swallows the `TypeError` when stamping a builtin, so the stamps the fallback relies on were never written, and `:433-442` then replaces the entry (same object → "refresh its tags in place and say nothing").
**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import register, get_registry, marks
R = get_registry()
register(dict, name="Mapping", role="sink", framework="py")
print("after register():        list_classes(role='sink'):", R.list_classes(role="sink"), "| marks(dict).role:", marks(dict).role)
R.register_class(dict, name="Mapping")        # a partial re-register (the shape register_class's docstring describes for a snapshot restore)
print("after partial re-register: list_classes(role='sink'):", R.list_classes(role="sink"), "| entry tags:", R._entries["Mapping"][0].tags)
```
**Actual output:**
```
after register():        list_classes(role='sink'): {'Mapping'} | marks(dict).role: None
after partial re-register: list_classes(role='sink'): set() | entry tags: (None, None, None, None, None)
```
**Expected:** the entry keeps `role=sink, framework=py`. AGENTS.md → "Behavioral marks": "Each mark follows the fallback template (argument wins, else the existing mark survives), so a partial re-register never drops tags"; `registry.py:270-273`: "a direct call (e.g. a snapshot restore) may forward as little as `name`/`category` — each mark falls back to the class's EXISTING mark … so a partial re-register never drops tags stamped earlier" — for a target that rejects stamps the registry ENTRY is the only place the tags exist, and it is the one place the fallback does not look. (`marks(dict).role: None` on the first line is the same blindness on the read side — BUGS-2026-08-19 R15 filed `is_configurable`'s face of it.)
**Known?** NEW (R15 of 2026-08-19 covers the read side; this is the write-side tag loss)
## R18 · S4 — A NON-callable registers (`register(42, name="Answer")`, `configurable(name="Kw")(42)`); the refusal comes only at construction, as `ConstructionError: … 'int' object is not callable`
**Where:** `confluid/decorators.py:185` — the `not callable(cls)` guard runs only on the POSITIONAL path (`cls is not None`); `:194-248` `decorator(c)` has no callable check, and `:255-334` `register()` / `registry.py:291-306` `register_class` check only for descriptors and a missing name — `hasattr(42, "__dict__")` is `False`, `name=` supplies the name, and the int is stored as `_ClassEntry.cls`.
**Repro:**
```python
from confluid import register, configurable, get_registry, load
R = get_registry()
for target, nm in ((42, "Answer"), (object(), "Obj")):
    try:
        register(target, name=nm); print(f"register({target!r}, name={nm!r}) -> registered; get_class:", R.get_class(nm))
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"register({target!r}) ->", type(e).__name__, e)
try:
    configurable(name="Kw")(42); print("configurable(name='Kw')(42) -> registered; get_class:", R.get_class("Kw"))
except Exception as e:
    print("configurable(name='Kw')(42) ->", type(e).__name__, e)
try:
    print(load("o: !class:Answer\n"))
except Exception as e:
    print("load('o: !class:Answer') ->", type(e).__name__ + ":", e)
```
**Actual output:**
```
register(42, name='Answer') -> registered; get_class: 42
register(<object object at 0x1022cce50>, name='Obj') -> registered; get_class: <object object at 0x1022cce50>
configurable(name='Kw')(42) -> registered; get_class: 42
load('o: !class:Answer') -> ConstructionError: Failed to construct 42 at <unicode string>:1:4: 'int' object is not callable
```
**Expected:** `ConfigurableDefinitionError` at registration naming the object. AGENTS.md → Registry: "registration REFUSES what it cannot name or bind … They used to … register a broken target" — a non-callable can never be bound; `decorators.py:189-192` already refuses the positional spelling ("configurable() takes the class or callable to register as its positional argument"), so the keyword spelling and `register()` disagree with it.
**Known?** NEW (BUGS-2026-08-19 R1/R3 fixed the positional-string and descriptor faces)
## R19 · S4 — `confluid-bake <subpackage>` and `confluid-bake <single module>` exit 0, print "updated", and write a table the engine never reads
**Where:** `confluid/bake.py:113-122` — `baked_module_path` writes `Path(package.__file__).parent / "_confluid_baked.py"` — for `bk2.sub` that is `bk2/sub/_confluid_baked.py`, for a plain module `single2` it is `./_confluid_baked.py`; `confluid/introspect.py:148-151` — `top = module.split(".", 1)[0]; importlib.import_module(f"{top}.{BAKED_MODULE_BASENAME}")` only ever imports `bk2._confluid_baked` / `single2._confluid_baked` (the latter impossible: `single2` is not a package). Neither side validates the other's assumption.
**Repro:**

pathlib.Path("bk2/sub").mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
pathlib.Path("bk2/__init__.py").write_text(""); pathlib.Path("bk2/sub/__init__.py").write_text("")
pathlib.Path("bk2/sub/ops.py").write_text("from confluid import configurable\n@configurable\nclass Op:\n    def __init__(self):\n        self.scale = 2\n")
pathlib.Path("single2.py").write_text("from confluid import configurable\n@configurable\nclass Solo:\n    def __init__(self):\n        self.width = 8\n")
sys.path.insert(0, ".")
for target in ("bk2.sub", "single2"):

    print(f"confluid-bake {target} -> exit {r.returncode}:", [l for l in r.stdout.splitlines() if "classes" in l])
from confluid.introspect import baked_init_attrs
import bk2.sub.ops, single2
print("bk2/sub/_confluid_baked.py written:", pathlib.Path("bk2/sub/_confluid_baked.py").exists(), "| baked_init_attrs(bk2.sub.ops.Op):", baked_init_attrs(bk2.sub.ops.Op))
print("./_confluid_baked.py written:", pathlib.Path("_confluid_baked.py").exists(), "| baked_init_attrs(single2.Solo):", baked_init_attrs(single2.Solo))
```
**Actual output:**
```
confluid-bake bk2.sub -> exit 0: ['bk2.sub: 1 classes -> /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-gert-taidal/7b14aa64-a2bc-4de0-847a-9e897af1e1a4/scratchpad/hunt/R/work/bk2/sub/_confluid_baked.py [updated]']
confluid-bake single2 -> exit 0: ['single2: 1 classes -> /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-gert-taidal/7b14aa64-a2bc-4de0-847a-9e897af1e1a4/scratchpad/hunt/R/work/_confluid_baked.py [updated]']
bk2/sub/_confluid_baked.py written: True | baked_init_attrs(bk2.sub.ops.Op): None
./_confluid_baked.py written: True | baked_init_attrs(single2.Solo): None
```
**Expected:** the bake either writes to the TOP package's table (merging) or refuses a non-top-level / non-package target with `ConfigurationError` naming the top package to bake instead. `bake.py:13-16`: "Run it in the packaging pipeline, BEFORE freezing/zipping:: python -m confluid.bake mypackage [otherpackage ...]" and `:8-10` "emits a generated `<package>/_confluid_baked.py` table that the engine unions in at runtime"; AGENTS.md → "packaged mode": "`confluid-bake <package>` … `<pkg>/_confluid_baked.py`, consulted per MRO class" — a build step that reports success and produces nothing the runtime consults is drift between the two halves of one mechanism.
**Known?** NEW
## R20 · S4 — Nested `@configurable` classes are never baked: after `confluid-bake`, a frozen deployment still loses their body slots and the warning tells the operator to run the bake they already ran
**Where:** `confluid/bake.py:92-98` — `for value in vars(module).values(): if not isinstance(value, type) or value.__module__ != module_name: continue` — only module-level names are scanned, so `Outer.Inner` (qualname `Outer.Inner`) is never keyed; the runtime scan (`introspect.py:583-606` `_body_slot_sources`) walks every class it is handed, nested or not, so dev and packaged diverge for exactly the classes the bake exists to cover.
**Repro:**
```python
import sys, subprocess, pathlib, py_compile, shutil
pathlib.Path("fz2").mkdir(exist_ok=True); pathlib.Path("fz2/__init__.py").write_text("")
pathlib.Path("fz2/mod.py").write_text(
    "from confluid import configurable\n"
    "@configurable\nclass Top:\n    def __init__(self, lr: float = 0.1):\n        self.lr = lr\n        self.momentum = 0.9\n"
    "class Outer:\n    @configurable\n    class Inner:\n        def __init__(self):\n            self.depth = 3\n")
subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-m", "confluid.bake", "fz2"], capture_output=True)
print("bake table:", pathlib.Path("fz2/_confluid_baked.py").read_text().split("= {")[1].strip())
py_compile.compile("fz2/mod.py", cfile="fz2/mod.pyc"); pathlib.Path("fz2/mod.py").unlink(); shutil.rmtree("fz2/__pycache__", ignore_errors=True)
code = ("import sys; sys.path.insert(0, '.')\nimport fz2.mod\nfrom confluid import load\n"
        "print('Top.momentum (baked, bare key 0.5):', load('o: !class:Top\\nmomentum: 0.5\\n')['o'].momentum)\n"
        "print('Inner.depth  (nested, bare key 7):', load('o: !class:Inner\\ndepth: 7\\n')['o'].depth)\n")
r = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-c", code], capture_output=True, text=True)
print("\n".join(l.split("| ", 2)[-1] if "| WARNING" in l else l for l in (r.stdout + r.stderr).splitlines() if "Loggair initialized" not in l))
```
**Actual output:**
```
bake table: 'fz2.mod.Top': ('lr', 'momentum',),
}
Top.momentum (baked, bare key 0.5): 0.5
Inner.depth  (nested, bare key 7): 3
confluid.broadcast:_warn_if_init_unscannable:342 | cannot scan __init__ body of fz2.mod.Outer.Inner (source unavailable — compiled/frozen?): post-init broadcast attrs are invisible; run 'confluid-bake <package>' at build time or declare @configurable(broadcast_attrs=[...])
```
(`Top` — the module-level class — proves the sourceless path works when the table has the entry: the bare key lands. `Inner` keeps its default `3` and the advice names the step that was just run.)
**Expected:** `'fz2.mod.Outer.Inner': ('depth',)` in the table and `Inner.depth == 7`. `bake.py:18-19`: "Every class the package defines with its own `__init__` is baked — not only `@configurable` ones"; docs/extending-discovery.md §8: "Body slots vanish in a frozen/zipped deployment | `inspect.getsource` fails, the AST scan is empty | run `confluid-bake <package>`". `scan_package`'s own docstring (`bake.py:73-74`) admits "every top-level class", contradicting the module docstring one screen above.
**Known?** NEW
## R21 · S4 — `confluid-bake nosuchpkg` prints a raw `ModuleNotFoundError` traceback
**Where:** `confluid/bake.py:77` — `package = importlib.import_module(package_name)` in `scan_package` has no guard; `:162` `main` calls it outside any `try`, so the CLI ends in the interpreter's traceback printer.
**Repro:**
```python
import sys, subprocess
r = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-m", "confluid.bake", "nosuchpkg"], capture_output=True, text=True)
print("exit", r.returncode); print("\n".join(r.stderr.splitlines()[-3:]))
```
**Actual output:**
```
exit 1
  File "<frozen importlib._bootstrap>", line 1360, in _find_and_load
  File "<frozen importlib._bootstrap>", line 1324, in _find_and_load_unlocked
ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'nosuchpkg'
```
(Full stderr is the 20-line traceback through `runpy`, `bake.main`, `bake_broadcast_attrs`, `scan_package`, `importlib`.)
**Expected:** one line on stderr (`confluid-bake: package 'nosuchpkg' is not importable (No module named 'nosuchpkg')`), exit 1 — the treatment `baked_module_path` already gives the namespace-package case via `ConfigurationError` (`bake.py:117-121`), and the treatment BUGS-2026-08-19 CD16 established for the other console script (`hydraide`). AGENTS.md → "Typed exceptions": "Every error confluid raises MUST come from `confluid/exceptions.py` (root `ConfluidError`), never a bare builtin".
**Known?** NEW

---

### Suspected, not reproduced — R

- **Concurrent registration of two identical-tag classes under one name** (`_store` has no lock; two threads passing the replace loop before either appends would yield two coexisting identical-tag entries and an `AmbiguousClassError` whose hint says "no single tag tells them apart") — 0/300 rounds with a `Barrier` and `sys.setswitchinterval(1e-6)`; the window did not open.
- **Registration racing enumeration** (3 registering + 3 enumerating threads, `list_classes`/`list_categories`/`list_groups`/`get_class`, 2 s, `setswitchinterval(1e-6)`) — 0 errors; the BUGS-2026-08-19 R9 snapshot fix holds.
- `@configurable(name="")` silently registers under the class's own name (`registry.py:298` `name or own_name or __name__`) — measured, not filed (an empty name is a spelling question, not a wrong registration).
- `DATA_ROOT=~/data` in `.env` is refused by `load_workspace_env` as "does not exist on this machine", and the loader's `$DATA_ROOT/x` substitution leaves `~//x` literal too — the two agree, so not filed as a divergence (a spelling-acceptance wish; PA27 expanded `~` for entry/include PATHS only).
- The docs/discovery.md `$engine` selector example logs `WARNING … has no attribute 'engine' … set as a post-init attribute anyway` once per class built — re-measured (three warnings for the two-class example); already in the 2026-08-19 suspected list (broadcast area).
- BUGS-2026-08-19 BC6 still reproduces unchanged (`secret=5` through `NoBroadcast`, `foo` absorbed, with `!class:CE@framework=$engine`) — open, not refiled.
- The `@role=None` / `get_class('Foo', role=None)` hint in `AmbiguousClassError` (visible in R1's output) — BUGS-2026-08-13 C12's second face, open, not refiled.
- `$key` selector whose key holds a literal `null` is reported as "could not be resolved — no key" (listed 2026-08-19) — not re-run.
- `marks()` on `None` / `42` / a module / `staticmethod` / `classmethod` / `object()` and `get_class(None | 42 | "" | b"x")` — all answer "not configurable" / `None` without raising; no defect.
- Re-registering a class with a DIFFERENT tag (`@configurable(role="loss")` then `register(A, role="metric")`) cleans the old index bucket (`role=loss → set()`), and an unregistered subclass answers `get_class(Sub) → None`, `key_for(Sub) → None` (BUGS-2026-08-19 R4 holds); `is_configurable(Sub) → True` is the known BUGS-2026-08-13 C11 face.

---

# Status of earlier reports' open entries, and what this hunt re-confirmed

**Independent re-verification.** Fourteen entries — two per area — were re-run from a fresh directory by the
consolidating reviewer before this file was written, against the same tree (`243aa03`): R3, R4, SR1, SR8, ENG-1,
ENG-9, BC1, BC2, N2, N7, PA1, PA8, CD1, CD3. All fourteen reproduced byte-for-byte.

**`BUGS-2026-08-19.md` — deferred by ruling, not refiled.** BC10 (a parent `'**'` rider merged into an own rider
inherits the own rider's earlier position) and BC11 (a code-born deferred slot is reached by root bare keys only) stay
in `TASKS.md` as documented limits under the scope ruling of 2026-08-20 (no per-key verdict or position bookkeeping on
the precedence engine). Nothing in this hunt depends on them.

**`BUGS-2026-08-19.md` — entries marked FIXED whose OTHER face still reproduces (filed here as new entries with the
overlap named):** CD14 ↔ CD16 (the undecodable-file face of the `hydraide` one-line contract), PA14 ↔ PA18 (the
`$$`-escape face of the document-stage idempotence breach), SR6 ↔ BUGS-2026-08-13 P3 (the marker/list-root face of
the unscoped load).

**`BUGS-2026-08-13.md` — still-open entries re-confirmed by this hunt's reviewers** (each carries a `Known?` line
naming it): E5 (ENG-20), E9 (ENG-19), P3 (SR6), P4 (PA26), P6 (PA9), P14 (PA15), C5 (CD28), I3 (N6, N23), I11 (N5);
CD7 and CD17 of the 2026-08-19 report are re-confirmed by CD29 and CD27.

**Scope ruling applied during review.** Reviewers were instructed not to file a refused ambiguous spelling, a wish for
new machinery, or a required constructor argument. Where an entry below asks for a located error instead of a silent
answer, that is the ruling's own remedy ("a located refusal or a documented limit"), not machinery.
